```python
"""
data_pipeline.py

Asynchronous data pipeline for the ML scalping engine.

Responsibilities (Task 2):
    1. Connect to Binance via CCXT (public endpoints, no API keys required).
    2. Discover the top N (default 20) USDT-quoted pairs ranked by 24h quote volume.
    3. Asynchronously fetch OHLCV candles for each pair on two timeframes (5m and 15m).
    4. Return a clean, structured dictionary of pandas DataFrames ready for
       feature engineering (Task 3).

Design notes:
    - Uses ccxt.async_support for true async I/O.
    - A semaphore + enableRateLimit keeps us well inside Binance public API limits.
    - Leveraged tokens (BTCUP/BTCDOWN etc.) and stable/stable pairs are filtered out
      because they are unsuitable scalping instruments.
    - Every network call is wrapped with retry logic and graceful failure handling:
      one bad symbol never kills the whole pipeline run.
"""

import asyncio
import logging
import time
from typing import Dict, List, Tuple

import ccxt.async_support as ccxt_async
import pandas as pd

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

TOP_N_PAIRS = 20          # how many top-volume USDT pairs to trade
CANDLE_LIMIT = 500        # candles per timeframe request (Binance max = 1000)
TIMEFRAMES = ("5m", "15m")
FETCH_CONCURRENCY = 5     # parallel OHLCV requests in flight
MAX_RETRIES = 3
RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS = 2.0

# Pairs whose base asset matches these are stable/stable or leveraged tokens
# and are excluded from the universe.
EXCLUDED_BASES = {
    "USDC", "FDUSD", "TUSD", "BUSD", "DAI", "EUR", "GBP", "TRY", "BRL",
    "AEUR", "USDP", "PAXG",
}
EXCLUDED_PATTERNS = ("UP", "DOWN", "BULL", "BEAR")  # leveraged token suffixes

logger = logging.getLogger("data_pipeline")
```

Let me write that logic:

```python
def _is_leveraged_token(base: str, all_bases: set) -> bool:
    """Detect Binance leveraged tokens (BTCUP, ETHDOWN, ...) without
    accidentally filtering legit coins like JUP."""
    for suffix in ("UP", "DOWN"):
        if base.endswith(suffix) and base[:-len(suffix)] in all_bases:
            return True
    return False
```

That's elegant.

Now the main pipeline:

```python
class BinanceDataPipeline:
    def __init__(self, top_n=TOP_N_PAIRS, timeframes=TIMEFRAMES, candle_limit=CANDLE_LIMIT):
        self.top_n = top_n
        self.timeframes = timeframes
        self.candle_limit = candle_limit
        self.exchange = ccxt_async.binance({"enableRateLimit": True})

    async def close(self):
        await self.exchange.close()

    async def fetch_top_usdt_pairs(self) -> List[str]:
        tickers = await self.exchange.fetch_tickers()
        usdt = {}
        for symbol, t in tickers.items():
            if not symbol.endswith("/USDT"):
                continue
            base = symbol.split("/")[0]
            if base in EXCLUDED_BASES:
                continue
            quote_vol = t.get("quoteVolume")
            if quote_vol is None:
                continue
            usdt[symbol] = float(quote_vol)
        # leveraged filter needs the set of all bases
        all_bases = {s.split("/")[0] for s in tickers}
        usdt = {s: v for s, v in usdt.items() if not _is_leveraged_token(s.split("/")[0], all_bases)}
        ranked = sorted(usdt.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1], reverse=True)
        top = [s for s, _ in ranked[: self.top_n]]
        return top
```

Hmm, fetch_tickers on Binance returns all tickers — that's one call, fine. Alternatively fetch_tickers with params. OK.

Then fetch OHLCV with retry:

```python
    async def _fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(self, symbol, timeframe):
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                ohlcv = await self.exchange.fetch_ohlcv(symbol, timeframe=timeframe, limit=self.candle_limit)
                return ohlcv
            except (ccxt_async.NetworkError, ccxt_async.ExchangeError) as exc:
                logger.warning("Attempt %d/%d failed for %s %s: %s", attempt, MAX_RETRIES, symbol, timeframe, exc)
                if attempt < MAX_RETRIES:
                    await asyncio.sleep(RETRY_DELAY_SECONDS * attempt)
        return None
```

Then convert to DataFrame:

```python
def ohlcv_to_dataframe(ohlcv) -> pd.DataFrame:
    df = pd.DataFrame(ohlcv, columns=["timestamp", "open", "high", "low", "close", "volume"])
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["timestamp"], unit="ms", utc=True)
    df.set_index("timestamp", inplace=True)
    for col in ("open", "high", "low", "close", "volume"):
        df[col] = pd.to_numeric(df[col], errors="coerce")
    df.dropna(inplace=True)
    return df
```

Then the gather with semaphore:

```python
    async def fetch_all_ohlcv(self, symbols):
        sem = asyncio.Semaphore(FETCH_CONCURRENCY)
        result: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame] = {}

        async def worker(symbol, timeframe):
            async with sem:
                raw = await self._fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(symbol, timeframe)
            if raw:
                result[(symbol, timeframe)] = ohlcv_to_dataframe(raw)
            else:
                logger.error("Giving up on %s %s", symbol, timeframe)

        tasks = [worker(s, tf) for s in symbols for tf in self.timeframes]
        await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
        return result
```

```python
"""
data_pipeline.py
================

Asynchronous market-data pipeline for the ML scalping engine.

Task 2 scope:
    1. Connect to Binance through CCXT (public endpoints only, no API keys).
    2. Rank all spot USDT pairs by 24h quote volume and keep the top N (20).
    3. Asynchronously download OHLCV candles for every selected pair on the
       5m and 15m timeframes.
    4. Convert everything into tidy pandas DataFrames indexed by UTC timestamp
       and return them in a structure that the feature-engineering module
       (next task) can consume directly.

Usage (standalone smoke test):
    python data_pipeline.py

Usage (from other modules):
    from data_pipeline import BinanceDataPipeline
    pipeline = BinanceDataPipeline()
    symbols, data = await pipeline.run()
    await pipeline.close()
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import ccxt.async_support as ccxt_async
import pandas as pd

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

TOP_N_PAIRS = 20
TIMEFRAMES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("5m", "15m")
CANDLE_LIMIT = 500          # candles per request (Binance allows up to 1000)
MAX_CONCURRENT_REQUESTS = 5 # parallel OHLCV fetches (rate-limit friendly)
MAX_RETRIES = 3
RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 2.0

# Stablecoin / fiat bases quoted against USDT are not tradable scalping
# instruments (no volatility), so they are removed from the universe.
EXCLUDED_BASES = {
    "USDC", "FDUSD", "TUSD", "BUSD", "DAI", "USDP", "PAXG", "AEUR",
    "EUR", "GBP", "TRY", "BRL", "JPY", "RUB", "UAH", "ZAR", "TRY",
}
```

```python
)
logger = logging.getLogger("data_pipeline")
```

Leveraged token detection:

```python
def _is_leveraged_token(base: str, all_bases: set) -> bool:
    """
    Binance leveraged tokens look like BTCUP / ETHDOWN / ADAUP ...
    A naive suffix check would also catch legitimate coins such as JUP,
    so we only flag a symbol when stripping the suffix leaves another
    listed base asset (BTCUP -> BTC exists => leveraged; JUP -> J does not).
    """
    for suffix in ("UP", "DOWN"):
        if base.endswith(suffix):
            stem = base[: -len(suffix)]
            if stem and stem in all_bases:
                return True
    return False
```

OHLCV conversion:

```python
def ohlcv_to_dataframe(ohlcv: list) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert a raw CCXT OHLCV list into a clean UTC-indexed DataFrame."""
    df = pd.DataFrame(
        ohlcv, columns=["timestamp", "open", "high", "low", "close", "volume"]
    )
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["timestamp"], unit="ms", utc=True)
    df = df.set_index("timestamp")
    df = df.apply(pd.to_numeric, errors="coerce")
    df = df.dropna()
    return df.sort_index()
```

Pipeline class:

```python
class BinanceDataPipeline:
    """
    Async pipeline:
        fetch_top_usdt_pairs()  -> top-N symbols by 24h quote volume
        fetch_all_ohlcv()       -> {(symbol, timeframe): DataFrame}
        run()                   -> (symbols, data) in one call
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        top_n: int = TOP_N_PAIRS,
        timeframes: Tuple[str, ...] = TIMEFRAMES,
        candle_limit: int = CANDLE_LIMIT,
    ) -> None:
        self.top_n = top_n
        self.timeframes = timeframes
        self.candle_limit = candle_limit
        self.exchange: ccxt_async.binance = ccxt_async.binance(
            {
                "enableRateLimit": True,   # CCXT throttles requests for us
                "options": {"defaultType": "spot"},
            }
        )

    # -- lifecycle ---------------------------------------------------------
    async def close(self) -> None:
        """Release the underlying aiohttp session (call when finished)."""
        await self.exchange.close()

    # -- universe selection -------------------------------------------------
    async def fetch_top_usdt_pairs(self) -> List[str]:
        logger.info("Fetching ticker snapshot to rank USDT pairs by volume ...")
        tickers = await self.exchange.fetch_tickers()

        all_bases = {symbol.split("/")[0] for symbol in tickers}
        volumes: Dict[str, float] = {}

        for symbol, ticker in tickers.items():
            if not symbol.endswith("/USDT"):
                continue
            base = symbol.split("/")[0]
            if base in EXCLUDED_BASES:
                continue
            if _is_leveraged_token(base, all_bases):
                continue
            quote_volume = ticker.get("quoteVolume")
            if quote_volume is None:
                continue
            volumes[symbol] = float(quote_volume)

        ranked = sorted(volumes.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1], reverse=True)
        top_symbols = [symbol for symbol, _ in ranked[: self.top_n]]

        logger.info("Selected top %d USDT pairs: %s", len(top_symbols), ", ".join(top_symbols))
        return top_symbols
```

Hmm, one issue: fetch_tickers for binance returns dict of all symbols; some may have None quoteVolume — handled.

OHLCV fetch with retry:

```python
    async def _fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(self, symbol: str, timeframe: str) -> Optional[list]:
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                return await self.exchange.fetch_ohlcv(
                    symbol, timeframe=timeframe, limit=self.candle_limit
                )
            except (ccxt_async.NetworkError, ccxt_async.ExchangeNotAvailable, ccxt_async.RequestTimeout) as exc:
                wait = RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS * attempt
                logger.warning(
                    "Retry %d/%d for %s %s after error: %s (sleeping %.1fs)",
                    attempt, MAX_RETRIES, symbol, timeframe, exc, wait,
                )
                await asyncio.sleep(wait)
            except ccxt_async.ExchangeError as exc:
                # Permanent problem (bad symbol, etc.) - do not retry.
                logger.error("Exchange rejected %s %s: %s", symbol, timeframe, exc)
                return None
        return None
```

Note: ccxt_async.ExchangeError is base of many; NetworkError is separate branch (NetworkError is not subclass of ExchangeError; both under BaseError). Order matters: catch specific ones. Actually RequestTimeout is subclass of NetworkError. Fine.

Fetch all:

```python
    async def fetch_all_ohlcv(self, symbols: List[str]) -> Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]:
        semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(MAX_CONCURRENT_REQUESTS)
        data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame] = {}

        async def worker(symbol: str, timeframe: str) -> None:
            async with semaphore:
                raw = await self._fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(symbol, timeframe)
            if raw:
                df = ohlcv_to_dataframe(raw)
                if len(df) >= self.candle_limit * 0.8:   # guard against thin history
                    data[(symbol, timeframe)] = df
                else:
                    logger.warning("%s %s: only %d candles returned, skipping", symbol, timeframe, len(df))
            else:
                logger.error("No OHLCV data for %s %s", symbol, timeframe)

        tasks = [worker(symbol, tf) for symbol in symbols for tf in self.timeframes]
        logger.info("Fetching %d OHLCV series (%d pairs x %d timeframes) ...", len(tasks), len(symbols), len(self.timeframes))
        await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
        logger.info("Completed %d/%d series.", len(data), len(tasks))
        return data
```

Hmm, the 0.8 guard: newly listed coins may have less history. For scalping features we want enough candles. 500*0.8=400. Reasonable. But maybe make it a min_candles param. Keep simple.

run():

```python
    async def run(self) -> Tuple[List[str], Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]]:
        symbols = await self.fetch_top_usdt_pairs()
        if not symbols:
            raise RuntimeError("No USDT pairs returned by Binance - aborting.")
        data = await self.fetch_all_ohlcv(symbols)
        return symbols, data
```

Convenience loader for other modules:

```python
async def load_market_data(top_n: int = TOP_N_PAIRS) -> Tuple[List[str], Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]]:
    """One-shot helper for downstream modules (feature engineering, ML)."""
    pipeline = BinanceDataPipeline(top_n=top_n)
    try:
        return await pipeline.run()
    finally:
        await pipeline.close()
```

Standalone main:

```python
async def _main() -> None:
    pipeline = BinanceDataPipeline()
    try:
        symbols, data = await pipeline.run()
    finally:
        await pipeline.close()

    print("\n" + "=" * 72)
    print(f"TOP {len(symbols)} BINANCE USDT PAIRS BY 24H QUOTE VOLUME")
    print("=" * 72)
    for i, symbol in enumerate(symbols, start=1):
        parts = []
        for tf in pipeline.timeframes:
            df = data.get((symbol, tf))
            if df is None:
                parts.append(f"{tf}: MISSING")
            else:
                last = df["close"].iloc[-1]
                parts.append(f"{tf}: {len(df)} candles (last close {last:.6g})")
        print(f"{i:>2}. {symbol:<14} " + " | ".join(parts))
    print("=" * 72)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted by user.")
```

```
# ============================================================================
# ML Scalping Engine - Python dependencies
# Install with:  pip install -r requirements.txt
```

## Task 2 — `data_pipeline.py`

Design highlights (full explanation below the code):

- **True async I/O** via `ccxt.async_support` — one HTTP session, many concurrent requests.
- **Rate-limit friendly**: `enableRateLimit=True` plus an `asyncio.Semaphore` capping concurrent OHLCV fetches at 5.
- **Smart universe filter**: removes stablecoin/fiat bases and Binance leveraged tokens — using a stem check (`BTCUP` → `BTC` exists → leveraged) so legitimate coins like `JUP` are **not** accidentally excluded.
- **Resilient**: per-request retries with linear backoff; permanent errors (e.g., bad symbol) fail fast without killing the run; thin-history series are dropped.
- **Clean output**: `{(symbol, timeframe): UTC-indexed DataFrame}` — exactly the structure the feature-engineering module will consume.

data_pipeline.py
```
"""
data_pipeline.py
================

Asynchronous market-data pipeline for the ML scalping engine.

Task 2 scope:
    1. Connect to Binance through CCXT (public endpoints only, no API keys).
    2. Rank all spot USDT pairs by 24h quote volume and keep the top N (20).
    3. Asynchronously download OHLCV candles for every selected pair on the
       5m and 15m timeframes.
    4. Convert everything into tidy pandas DataFrames indexed by UTC timestamp
       and return them in a structure the feature-engineering module (next
       task) can consume directly.

Usage (standalone smoke test):
    python data_pipeline.py

Usage (from other modules):
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import ccxt.async_support as ccxt_async
import pandas as pd

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

TOP_N_PAIRS = 20            # how many top-volume USDT pairs to select
TIMEFRAMES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("5m", "15m")
CANDLE_LIMIT = 500          # candles per request (Binance allows up to 1000)
MAX_CONCURRENT_REQUESTS = 5 # parallel OHLCV fetches in flight (rate-limit safe)
MAX_RETRIES = 3             # retries per OHLCV request
RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS = 2.0 # linear backoff base: 2s, 4s, 6s ...
MIN_CANDLES_RATIO = 0.8     # drop series with < 80% of requested candles

# Stablecoin / fiat bases quoted against USDT have no volatility and are
# useless as scalping instruments, so they are removed from the universe.
EXCLUDED_BASES = {
    "USDC", "FDUSD", "TUSD", "BUSD", "DAI", "USDP", "PAXG", "AEUR",
    "EUR", "GBP", "TRY", "BRL", "JPY", "RUB", "UAH", "ZAR",
}

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("data_pipeline")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _is_leveraged_token(base: str, all_bases: set) -> bool:
    """
    Detect Binance leveraged tokens (BTCUP, ETHDOWN, ADAUP, ...).

    A naive suffix check would also catch legitimate coins such as JUP,
    so we only flag a symbol when stripping the suffix leaves another
    listed base asset:
        BTCUP  -> BTC exists  => leveraged (excluded)
        JUP    -> J does not  => legitimate (kept)
    """
    for suffix in ("UP", "DOWN"):
        if base.endswith(suffix):
            stem = base[: -len(suffix)]
            if stem and stem in all_bases:
                return True
    return False


def ohlcv_to_dataframe(ohlcv: list) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Convert a raw CCXT OHLCV list into a clean, UTC-indexed DataFrame."""
    df = pd.DataFrame(
        ohlcv, columns=["timestamp", "open", "high", "low", "close", "volume"]
    )
    df["timestamp"] = pd.to_datetime(df["timestamp"], unit="ms", utc=True)
    df = df.set_index("timestamp")
    df = df.apply(pd.to_numeric, errors="coerce")
    df = df.dropna().sort_index()
    return df


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pipeline
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

class BinanceDataPipeline:
    """
    Async pipeline exposing three stages:

        fetch_top_usdt_pairs() -> top-N symbols ranked by 24h quote volume
        fetch_all_ohlcv()      -> {(symbol, timeframe): DataFrame}
        run()                  -> (symbols, data) in a single call
    """

    def __init__(
        self,
        top_n: int = TOP_N_PAIRS,
        timeframes: Tuple[str, ...] = TIMEFRAMES,
        candle_limit: int = CANDLE_LIMIT,
    ) -> None:
        self.top_n = top_n
        self.timeframes = timeframes
        self.candle_limit = candle_limit
        self.exchange = ccxt_async.binance(
            {
                "enableRateLimit": True,            # CCXT throttles for us
                "options": {"defaultType": "spot"}, # spot market only
            }
        )

    # -- lifecycle -----------------------------------------------------------

    async def close(self) -> None:
        """Release the underlying aiohttp session. Call when finished."""
        await self.exchange.close()

    # -- stage 1: universe selection ------------------------------------------

    async def fetch_top_usdt_pairs(self) -> List[str]:
        """Return the top-N USDT spot pairs ranked by 24h quote volume."""
        logger.info("Fetching ticker snapshot to rank USDT pairs by volume ...")
        await self.exchange.load_markets()
        tickers = await self.exchange.fetch_tickers()

        all_bases = {symbol.split("/")[0] for symbol in tickers}
        volumes: Dict[str, float] = {}

        for symbol, ticker in tickers.items():
            # Keep spot USDT-quoted pairs only (':' would indicate a swap).
            if ":" in symbol or not symbol.endswith("/USDT"):
                continue
            base = symbol.split("/")[0]
            if base in EXCLUDED_BASES or _is_leveraged_token(base, all_bases):
                continue
            quote_volume = ticker.get("quoteVolume")
            if quote_volume is None:
                continue
            volumes[symbol] = float(quote_volume)

        ranked = sorted(volumes.items(), key=lambda kv: kv[1], reverse=True)
        top_symbols = [symbol for symbol, _ in ranked[: self.top_n]]

        logger.info(
            "Selected top %d USDT pairs: %s",
            len(top_symbols), ", ".join(top_symbols),
        )
        return top_symbols

    # -- stage 2: OHLCV download ----------------------------------------------

    async def _fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(
        self, symbol: str, timeframe: str
    ) -> Optional[list]:
        """Fetch one OHLCV series with retries; permanent errors fail fast."""
        for attempt in range(1, MAX_RETRIES + 1):
            try:
                return await self.exchange.fetch_ohlcv(
                    symbol, timeframe=timeframe, limit=self.candle_limit
                )
            except ccxt_async.NetworkError as exc:
                # Transient (timeout, DDoS guard, rate limit) -> retry.
                wait = RETRY_BACKOFF_SECONDS * attempt
                logger.warning(
                    "Retry %d/%d for %s %s: %s (sleeping %.1fs)",
                    attempt, MAX_RETRIES, symbol, timeframe, exc, wait,
                )
                await asyncio.sleep(wait)
            except ccxt_async.ExchangeError as exc:
                # Permanent (bad symbol, market closed, ...) -> give up now.
                logger.error("Exchange rejected %s %s: %s", symbol, timeframe, exc)
                return None
        return None

    async def fetch_all_ohlcv(
        self, symbols: List[str]
    ) -> Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]:
        """Fetch OHLCV for every (symbol, timeframe) pair concurrently."""
        semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(MAX_CONCURRENT_REQUESTS)
        data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame] = {}

        async def worker(symbol: str, timeframe: str) -> None:
            async with semaphore:
                raw = await self._fetch_ohlcv_with_retry(symbol, timeframe)
            if not raw:
                logger.error("No OHLCV data for %s %s", symbol, timeframe)
                return
            df = ohlcv_to_dataframe(raw)
            min_candles = int(self.candle_limit * MIN_CANDLES_RATIO)
            if len(df) < min_candles:
                logger.warning(
                    "%s %s: only %d candles (need %d), skipping",
                    symbol, timeframe, len(df), min_candles,
                )
                return
            data[(symbol, timeframe)] = df

        tasks = [
            worker(symbol, timeframe)
            for symbol in symbols
            for timeframe in self.timeframes
        ]
        logger.info(
            "Fetching %d OHLCV series (%d pairs x %d timeframes) ...",
            len(tasks), len(symbols), len(self.timeframes),
        )
        await asyncio.gather(*tasks)
        logger.info("Completed %d/%d series.", len(data), len(tasks))
        return data

    # -- orchestration ---------------------------------------------------------

    async def run(self) -> Tuple[List[str], Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]]:
        """Execute the full pipeline and return (symbols, data)."""
        symbols = await self.fetch_top_usdt_pairs()
        if not symbols:
            raise RuntimeError("No USDT pairs returned by Binance - aborting.")
        data = await self.fetch_all_ohlcv(symbols)
        return symbols, data


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Convenience entry points
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

async def load_market_data(
    top_n: int = TOP_N_PAIRS,
) -> Tuple[List[str], Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame]]:
    """One-shot helper for downstream modules (feature engineering, ML)."""
    pipeline = BinanceDataPipeline(top_n=top_n)
    try:
        return await pipeline.run()
    finally:
        await pipeline.close()


async def _main() -> None:
    """Standalone smoke test: run the pipeline and print a summary report."""
    pipeline = BinanceDataPipeline()
    try:
        symbols, data = await pipeline.run()
    finally:
        await pipeline.close()

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print(f"TOP {len(symbols)} BINANCE USDT PAIRS BY 24H QUOTE VOLUME")
    print("=" * 78)
    for i, symbol in enumerate(symbols, start=1):
        parts = []
        for timeframe in pipeline.timeframes:
            df = data.get((symbol, timeframe))
            if df is None:
                parts.append(f"{timeframe}: MISSING")
            else:
                last_close = df["close"].iloc[-1]
                parts.append(f"{timeframe}: {len(df)} candles (last {last_close:.6g})")
        print(f"{i:>2}. {symbol:<14} " + " | ".join(parts))
    print("=" * 78)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted by user.")
```

## How the code works

**`fetch_top_usdt_pairs()` — universe selection**
1. `load_markets()` + `fetch_tickers()` is a *single* API call returning every spot ticker with its 24h `quoteVolume`.
2. Filters in order: spot-only (`:` excludes perpetuals), USDT-quoted, not a stablecoin/fiat base, not a leveraged token.
3. Sorts by quote volume descending and slices the top 20. Volume ranking is re-computed on every run, so the universe adapts automatically — no hardcoded symbol list.

**`fetch_all_ohlcv()` — concurrent download**
- Builds one coroutine per `(symbol, timeframe)` → 40 tasks for 20 pairs × 2 timeframes.
- The semaphore allows only 5 in flight; combined with `enableRateLimit=True`, this stays comfortably inside Binance's public weight limits (1200 weight/min; `fetch_ohlcv` costs 1–2).
- `asyncio.gather` runs everything; a failure in one task never affects the others.

**`_fetch_ohlcv_with_retry()` — resilience**
- `NetworkError` (timeouts, DDoS guard, rate limit) → retried with linear backoff (2s, 4s, 6s).
- `ExchangeError` (invalid symbol, etc.) → permanent, logged, and skipped immediately.

**Output contract for the next stage**
- `data[("BTC/USDT", "5m")]` → DataFrame with a UTC DatetimeIndex and columns `open, high, low, close, volume` — directly consumable by `pandas_ta` in the feature-engineering task.
- Series with < 80% of the requested candles (freshly listed coins) are dropped so the ML feature matrix never contains truncated lookback windows.

**One honest note on the 95% target:** the ML engine will output calibrated *probabilities*, and we will surface them as the confidence percentage you asked for. No model can *guarantee* 95% win rate in live markets — but proper label design, probability calibration, and the news-based risk filter will get the signal quality as close to that goal as the data allows. I'll flag calibration quality explicitly when we evaluate the model.

**To smoke-test now:**
```
pip install -r requirements.txt
python data_pipeline.py
```

```python
def _swing_levels(df, lookback=2, confirm=2):
    # a bar is a swing high if its high is the max within [t-lookback, t+lookback]
    high = df["high"]; low = df["low"]
    win = 2*lookback + 1
    sh = high.rolling(win, center=True).max()
    swing_high = (high == sh)
    ...
```
Then to make it lookahead-safe: the swing at bar t is confirmed at t+lookback. So create a series of swing high *prices* indexed at t+lookback (confirmation time), then forward-fill. Implementation:

```python
swing_high_price = high.where(swing_high)  # NaN except at swing bars
confirmed = swing_high_price.shift(lookback)  # value appears at confirmation bar
last_swing_high = confirmed.ffill()
```
Then features: `dist_to_swing_high = (close - last_swing_high) / close` (negative below resistance, positive above = breakout). Similarly for lows. Also count of swings in recent window maybe. Keep it simple: distance features + boolean "above/below".

Rolling pivot points: classic floor pivots computed from previous rolling window:
```python
window = pivot_window (e.g., 12 bars = 1 hour on 5m)
prev_high = high.rolling(window).max().shift(1)
prev_low = low.rolling(window).min().shift(1)
prev_close = close.shift(1)
P = (prev_high + prev_low + prev_close) / 3
R1 = 2P - prev_low; S1 = 2P - prev_high
R2 = P + (prev_high - prev_low); S2 = P - (prev_high - prev_low)
```

```python
def sentiment_series(posts, index, bin_minutes=15):
    if not posts: return pd.Series(0.0, index=index), pd.Series(0, index=index)
    df = pd.DataFrame([...published_at, score...])
    df["published_at"] = pd.to_datetime(..., utc=True)
    df = df.set_index("published_at").sort_index()
    binned = df["score"].resample(f"{bin_minutes}min").mean()
    count = df["score"].resample(f"{bin_minutes}min").count()
    # reindex onto feature index with ffill; news known at publish time
    s = binned.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0.0)
    c = count.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0).astype(int)
```

Support/resistance:

```python
def _swing_series(series: pd.Series, is_high: bool, lookback: int) -> pd.Series:
    """Boolean mask of fractal swing points (centered window)."""
    win = 2 * lookback + 1
    if is_high:
        extremum = series.rolling(win, center=True).max()
        return series == extremum
    extremum = series.rolling(win, center=True).min()
    return series == extremum


def compute_support_resistance(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    out = df
    high, low, close = out["high"], out["low"], out["close"]
    atr = out["atr"]

    # --- rolling pivot points (classic floor pivots on the *previous* window)
    ph = high.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).max().shift(1)
    pl = low.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).min().shift(1)
    pc = close.shift(1)
    pivot = (ph + pl + pc) / 3.0
    rng = (ph - pl).replace(0, np.nan)
    out["pivot"] = pivot
    out["dist_pivot_atr"] = (close - pivot) / atr
    out["dist_r1_atr"] = (close - (2 * pivot - pl)) / atr
    out["dist_s1_atr"] = (close - (2 * pivot - ph)) / atr
    out["dist_r2_atr"] = (close - (pivot + rng)) / atr
    out["dist_s2_atr"] = (close - (pivot - rng)) / atr

    # --- swing highs / lows (fractal, confirmed after `SWING_LOOKBACK` bars)
    for side, series, is_high in (("high", high, True), ("low", low, False)):
        mask = _swing_series(series, is_high, SWING_LOOKBACK)
        swing_price = series.where(mask)
        # shift forward so the level is only visible once confirmed
        confirmed = swing_price.shift(SWING_LOOKBACK)
        last_swing = confirmed.ffill()
        out[f"dist_swing_{side}_atr"] = (close - last_swing) / atr
        out[f"swing_{side}_distance_pct"] = (close - last_swing).abs() / close
```
Hmm two features per side is redundant; keep `dist_swing_high_atr` and `dist_swing_low_atr` only. Also maybe `breakout_high` boolean: close > last_swing_high. I'll include dist only plus a boolean `above_swing_high` = (close > last_swing).astype(float)? The dist sign already encodes that. Keep dist only. Also add `swing_count_recent`? Skip.

Wait: pivot distances normalized by atr — atr NaN early → NaN. Fine.

Also note: `out` mutation — I'm passing df and adding columns; better to copy inside. I'll have `compute_timeframe_features(df)` that copies once and calls sub-functions operating on the same copy.

```python
def compute_timeframe_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    features = df.copy()
    compute_core_indicators(features)   # operates in place on the copy
    compute_momentum... 
```
I'll just make each helper take and return the df, or mutate in place with clear docstrings. In-place on a private copy is fine; I'll write helpers that mutate `df` in place and document it.

Multi-TF merge:

```python
def merge_higher_timeframe(base: pd.DataFrame, higher: pd.DataFrame, higher_tf: str) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Align higher-TF features onto the base index without lookahead."""
    shifted = higher.shift(1)  # higher-TF bar is only complete one bar later
    aligned = shifted.reindex(base.index, method="ffill")
    aligned.columns = [f"{higher_tf}_{col}" for col in aligned.columns]
    return pd.concat([base, aligned], axis=1)
```

Wait — subtlety: `higher.shift(1)` shifts by one 15m bar. The 15m bar stamped T covers [T, T+15). After shift(1), the value of bar T appears at timestamp T+15. Base 5m rows at T+15, T+20, T+25, T+30... reindex ffill: row T+15 gets bar T's features (bar T closed exactly at T+15). Row T+10 gets bar T-15's features. Correct, no lookahead. 

But careful: reindex with method='ffill' requires the higher index sorted and works when base timestamps don't exactly match — ffill fills from the most recent higher-TF timestamp ≤ base timestamp. Since 5m and 15m grids align (both Binance UTC-aligned), T+15 exists in base. Good.

```python
def build_feature_matrix(data, symbol, base_timeframe=BASE_TIMEFRAME, higher_timeframes=HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES) -> Optional[pd.DataFrame]:
    base_key = (symbol, base_timeframe)
    if base_key not in data: warn, return None
    base = compute_timeframe_features(data[base_key])
    for tf in higher_timeframes:
        key = (symbol, tf)
        if key not in data: warn; continue
        higher = compute_timeframe_features(data[key])
        base = merge_higher_timeframe(base, higher, tf)
    return base

def build_all_feature_matrices(data) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    symbols = sorted({sym for sym, tf in data})
    return {s: m for s in symbols if (m := build_feature_matrix(data, s)) is not None}
```
Walrus in dict comprehension — fine for py3.8+. Actually walrus in comprehension value position: `{s: m for s in symbols if (m := ...) is not None}` — valid.

Also `dropna` note: I won't dropna inside; expose `matrix.dropna()` to ML stage. Maybe provide param `dropna=False` in build_feature_matrix. I'll add `dropna: bool = False`.

score_title:
```python
def score_title(title: str) -> float:
    words = re.findall(r"[a-z']+", title.lower())
    if not words: return 0.0
    pos = sum(1 for w in words if w in POSITIVE_WORDS)
    neg = sum(1 for w in NEGATIVE_WORDS if w in NEGATIVE_WORDS)
```
Wait, multi-word phrases like "all-time high" won't match single tokens. Use substring phrase check separately:
```python
    t = title.lower()
    pos = sum(t.count(w) for w in POSITIVE_PHRASES)  # hmm counting overlaps
```
Simpler: token-based single words + a few multiword phrases checked via `in`. I'll do:
```python
def score_title(title: str) -> float:
    text = title.lower()
    pos = sum(1 for phrase in POSITIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    neg = sum(1 for phrase in NEGATIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    total = pos + neg
    if total == 0:
        return 0.0
    return (pos - neg) / total
```
With phrases as lowercase substrings: "surge", "rally", "bullish", "soar", "jumps", "breakout", "adoption", "partnership", "upgrade", "approval", "approves", "all-time high", "record high", "inflow", "accumulat", "gain", "beat", "boost", "surges", "pump", "moon", "buy the dip"...

negatives: "crash", "plunge", "plummet", "dump", "bearish", "hack", "hacked", "exploit", "breach", "lawsuit", "sues", "sued", "ban", "bans", "banned", "fraud", "liquidation", "liquidated", "sell-off", "selloff", "slump", "tumble", "sink", "drop", "falls", "fear", "fud", "warning", "warns", "down", "loss", "outflow", "ponzi", "rug pull", "delist", "probe", "investigation", "charged", "arrest", "scam".

Note "down"/"drop"/"falls" may cause false positives ("downgrade" is negative anyway). Fine for lexicon approach; document as heuristic.

```python
def _fetch_cryptopanic(currency: str, token: str) -> List[NewsPost]:
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    session = requests.Session()
    for page in range(1, MAX_PAGES + 1):
        params = {"auth_token": token, "currencies": currency.upper(), "public": "true"}
        if page > 1 and last_url: params["page"]... 
```
CryptoPanic pagination: response has "next" URL. Simpler: request page param. Free v1 supports `page`? It supports cursor via "next" URL. I'll just fetch page 1 and, if `next` present, follow it up to MAX_PAGES. Implement:

```python
    url: Optional[str] = CRYPTOPANIC_URL
    params = {"auth_token": token, "currencies": currency.upper(), "public": "true"}
    for _ in range(MAX_PAGES):
        resp = session.get(url, params=params, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT)
        resp.raise_for_status()
        payload = resp.json()
        for item in payload.get("results", []):
            posts.append(NewsPost(
                published_at=pd.to_datetime(item["published_at"], utc=True),
                title=item.get("title", ""),
                source=(item.get("source") or {}).get("title", "cryptopanic"),
                currencies=tuple(c.get("code", "") for c in item.get("currencies", [])),
            ))
        url = payload.get("next")
        params = None
        if not url:
            break
    return posts
```
Wrap caller with tenacity retry? tenacity is in requirements. Use `@retry(stop=stop_after_attempt(3), wait=wait_fixed(2), reraise=True)` on the network function. But careful: retrying on HTTPError for 401 etc. wastes time; reraise and catch outside. I'll apply tenacity to transient errors only: `retry(retry=retry_if_exception_type((requests.ConnectionError, requests.Timeout)), stop=..., wait=...)`. Good use of the dependency.

RSS fetch:
```python
def _fetch_rss(keywords: Sequence[str]) -> List[NewsPost]:
    posts = []
    for feed_url in RSS_FEEDS:
        try:
            parsed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning(...); continue
        for entry in parsed.entries[:50]:
            title = getattr(entry, "title", "")
            if not title: continue
            tl = title.lower()
            if not any(kw in tl for kw in keywords): continue
            ts = entry.get("published_parsed") or entry.get("updated_parsed")
            if ts:
                published = pd.Timestamp(datetime.fromtimestamp(time.mktime(ts), tz=timezone.utc))
            else:
                continue  # can't align without a timestamp
            posts.append(NewsPost(published, title, feed_url, ()))
    return posts
```
`time.mktime` interprets struct_time as local time — feedparser's published_parsed is UTC struct_time. Better: `datetime(*ts[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc)`. Use `pd.Timestamp(*ts[:6], tz="UTC")`. Yes: `pd.Timestamp(year=ts.tm_year, month=..., ...)`. Simplest: `pd.Timestamp(datetime(*ts[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc))`.

Cache:
```python
_cache: Dict[str, Tuple[float, List[NewsPost]]] = {}

def get_posts_for_asset(base: str, use_cache: bool = True) -> List[NewsPost]:
    key = base.upper()
    now = time.time()
    if use_cache and key in _cache and now - _cache[key][0] < CACHE_TTL_SECONDS:
        return _cache[key][1]
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    token = os.getenv(CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV)
    if token:
        try:
            posts = _fetch_cryptopanic(key, token)
            logger.info("CryptoPanic returned %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("CryptoPanic failed for %s (%s); falling back to RSS", key, exc)
    if not posts:
        keywords = ALIASES.get(key, (key.lower(),))
        try:
            posts = _fetch_rss(keywords)
            logger.info("RSS fallback returned %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("RSS fallback failed for %s: %s", key, exc)
            posts = []
    _cache[key] = (now, posts)
    return posts
```
If no token and RSS fails → empty → neutral. Also if no token at all and RSS returns nothing → neutral. Log once that no token is set:
```python

        logger.info("No %s set - using free RSS fallback (neutral scores if unavailable).", CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV)
```

```python
def sentiment_features(posts: Sequence[NewsPost], index: pd.DatetimeIndex) -> pd.DataFrame:
    cols = {"sentiment_score": 0.0, "news_count": 0}
    if not posts or len(index) == 0:
        return pd.DataFrame({"sentiment_score": pd.Series(0.0, index=index), "news_count": pd.Series(0, index=index)})
    df = pd.DataFrame({
        "published_at": [p.published_at for p in posts],
        "score": [score_title(p.title) for p in posts],
    }).set_index("published_at").sort_index()
    # keep only posts within the past NEWS_MAX_AGE relative to the last index ts? 
    rolling_score = df["score"].rolling(SENTIMENT_WINDOW).mean()
    rolling_count = df["score"].rolling(NEWS_COUNT_WINDOW).count()
    score = rolling_score.reindex(index.union(rolling_score.index)).ffill().reindex(index).fillna(0.0)
```
Hmm — time-based rolling on df["score"] with a datetime index: `df["score"].rolling('2h').mean()` works (index must be datetime, monotonic — sorted). Result index = post timestamps. To align onto candle index: `reindex(index, method="ffill")` — but reindex with method requires the source index monotonic increasing; it is. However reindex-ffill only fills values for target timestamps ≥ first source timestamp; earlier ones → NaN → fillna(0). But there's a subtlety: reindex(method='ffill') picks the last source value ≤ target. For target timestamps between posts, that's the rolling mean at the most recent post — which is the trailing 2h mean as of that post. Slightly stale between posts but fine. Alternative: resample to bins then reindex. I'll do the rolling approach; simpler and smooth enough. Actually, better: combine both — compute rolling on posts, then `reindex(index, method="ffill")`, `fillna(0.0)`.

Count: same with count → int, fillna(0).

Return DataFrame with columns ["sentiment_score", "news_count"].

```python
def attach_sentiment(features: pd.DataFrame, base: str, market_posts: Optional[List[NewsPost]] = None) -> pd.DataFrame:
    index = features.index
    asset_posts = get_posts_for_asset(base)
    asset_sent = sentiment_features(asset_posts, index)
    if market_posts is None:
        market_posts = get_posts_for_asset(MARKET_PROXY_ASSET) if base.upper() != MARKET_PROXY_ASSET else asset_posts
    market_sent = sentiment_features(market_posts, index)
    out = features.copy()
    out["sentiment_asset"] = asset_sent["sentiment_score"]
    out["sentiment_market"] = market_sent["sentiment_score"]
    out["news_count_1h"] = asset_sent["news_count"]
    # combined: prefer asset-specific, fall back to market proxy
    out["sentiment_combined"] = out["sentiment_asset"].where(
        out["news_count_1h"] > 0, out["sentiment_market"]
    )
    return out
```
Hmm `where(cond, other)` — keeps asset score where news_count>0 else market. Good.

Actually rolling operations skip NaNs in the sense that the mean is computed over non-NaN values in the window, and result is NaN only if count of non-NaN < min_periods. With min_periods=1, windows with at least one non-NaN give the mean of those. Good.

feature_engineering.py:

```python
"""
feature_engineering.py
======================

Task 3 - Feature engineering for the ML scalping engine.

Consumes the tidy OHLCV DataFrames produced by ``data_pipeline.py``
(keys are ``(symbol, timeframe)`` tuples, values are UTC-indexed DataFrames
with ``open/high/low/close/volume`` columns) and builds a per-symbol feature
matrix ready for the ML stage.

Feature groups
--------------
Per timeframe (5m base, 15m higher):
    * RSI(14)
    * MACD(12, 26, 9): line, signal, histogram
    * ATR(14) and ATR as % of price
    * EMA(9, 21, 50, 200) plus close-vs-EMA distance in %
    * Bollinger Bands(20, 2): lower/mid/upper, bandwidth, %B
    * VWAP (daily anchored) and close-vs-VWAP distance in %
    * Multi-horizon returns (1, 3, 5 bars) and realized volatility
    * Volume z-score and volume ratio
    * Candle anatomy: body and wick as % of the bar range
    * Dynamic support/resistance:
        - classic floor pivot points (P, R1, S1, R2, S2) computed on a
          *rolling previous* window (no lookahead), distances in ATR units
        - fractal swing highs/lows, surfaced only after confirmation,
          distances in ATR units
    * Cyclical time-of-day encoding (sin/cos)

Multi-timeframe handling
------------------------
The 15m feature block is aligned onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift and
forward-fill, so a 5m row never sees 15m information from a bar that has not
closed yet (no lookahead). Higher-timeframe columns are prefixed ``15m_``.

Usage
-----
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    from feature_engineering import build_all_feature_matrices

    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)   # {symbol: DataFrame}

Standalone smoke test (hits the Binance public API):
    python feature_engineering.py
"""
```

```python
from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pandas_ta as ta

from data_pipeline import load_market_data  # noqa: F401  (used by smoke test)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration

BASE_TIMEFRAME = "5m"
HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("15m",)

EMA_LENGTHS: Tuple[int, ...] = (9, 21, 50, 200)
RSI_LENGTH = 14
MACD_FAST, MACD_SLOW, MACD_SIGNAL = 12, 26, 9
ATR_LENGTH = 14
BB_LENGTH, BB_STD = 20, 2.0
VWAP_ANCHOR = "D"

RETURN_LAGS: Tuple[int, ...] = (1, 3, 5)
VOLATILITY_WINDOW = 12          # bars for realized volatility of 1-bar returns
VOLUME_WINDOW = 20              # rolling window for volume z-score / ratio

SWING_LOOKBACK = 3              # bars on each side for fractal swing points
PIVOT_WINDOW = 12               # rolling window for floor pivots (1h on 5m)

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("feature_engineering")
```

Helpers:

```python
def _macd_columns(close: pd.Series) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """MACD line, signal and histogram with version-tolerant column access."""
    macd = ta.macd(close, fast=MACD_FAST, slow=MACD_SLOW, signal=MACD_SIGNAL)
    if macd is None or macd.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "macd": np.nan,
                "macd_signal": np.nan,
                "macd_hist": np.nan,
            },
            index=close.index,
        )
    expected = {
        "macd": f"MACD_{MACD_FAST}_{MACD_SLOW}_{MACD_SIGNAL}",
        "macd_signal": f"MACDs_{MACD_FAST}_{MACD_SLOW}_{MACD_SIGNAL}",
        "macd_hist": f"MACDh_{MACD_FAST}_{MACD_SLOW}_{MACD_SIGNAL}",
    }
    out = {}
    for name, col in expected.items():
        out[name] = macd[col] if col in macd.columns else macd.iloc[:, 0]
```

Core indicators (mutates df in place):

```python
def _add_core_indicators(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """Add RSI, MACD, ATR, EMAs, Bollinger Bands and VWAP (in place)."""
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    volume = df["volume"]

    # -- momentum -----------------------------------------------------------
    df["rsi"] = ta.rsi(close, length=RSI_LENGTH)

    macd = _macd_columns(close)
    df["macd"] = macd["macd"]
    df["macd_signal"] = macd["macd_signal"]
    df["macd_hist"] = macd["macd_hist"]

    # -- volatility ----------------------------------------------------------
    atr = ta.atr(high, low, close, length=ATR_LENGTH)
    df["atr"] = atr
    df["atr_pct"] = atr / close

    bb = ta.bbands(close, length=BB_LENGTH, std=BB_STD)
    if bb is not None and not bb.empty:
        # pandas-ta 0.3.14b0 column order: BBL, BBM, BBU, BBB(bandwidth), BBP(%B)
        df["bb_lower"] = bb.iloc[:, 0]
        df["bb_mid"] = bb.iloc[:, 1]
        df["bb_upper"] = bb.iloc[:, 2]
        df["bb_bandwidth"] = bb.iloc[:, 3]
        df["bb_percent"] = bb.iloc[:, 4]
    else:
        for col in ("bb_lower", "bb_mid", "bb_upper", "bb_bandwidth", "bb_percent"):
            df[col] = np.nan

    # -- trend ----------------------------------------------------------------
    for length in EMA_LENGTHS:
        ema = ta.ema(close, length=length)
        df[f"ema_{length}"] = ema
        df[f"close_ema_{length}_pct"] = close / ema - 1.0 if ema is not None else np.nan
```

```python

            df[f"ema_{length}"] = ema
            df[f"close_ema_{length}_pct"] = close / ema - 1.0
        else:
            df[f"ema_{length}"] = np.nan
            df[f"close_ema_{length}_pct"] = np.nan
```

Returns/volume/anatomy:

```python
def _add_price_volume_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    open_, volume = df["open"], df["volume"]

    for lag in RETURN_LAGS:
        df[f"ret_{lag}"] = close.pct_change(lag)
    df["realized_vol"] = df["ret_1"].rolling(VOLATILITY_WINDOW).std()

    vol_mean = volume.rolling(VOLUME_WINDOW).mean()
    vol_std = volume.rolling(VOLUME_WINDOW).std()
    df["volume_zscore"] = (volume - vol_mean) / vol_std.replace(0.0, np.nan)
    df["volume_ratio"] = volume / vol_mean.replace(0.0, np.nan)

    bar_range = (high - low).replace(0.0, np.nan)
    df["body_pct"] = (close - open_) / bar_range
    df["upper_wick_pct"] = (high - df[["open", "close"]].max(axis=1)) / bar_range
    df["lower_wick_pct"] = (df[["open", "close"]].min(axis=1) - low) / bar_range

    # Cyclical time-of-day encoding (scalping activity is session dependent).
    minutes = df.index.hour * 60 + df.index.minute
    angle = 2.0 * np.pi * minutes / 1440.0
    df["tod_sin"] = np.sin(angle)
    df["tod_cos"] = np.cos(angle)
```
`df.index.hour` works on DatetimeIndex. minutes is Int64Index/array — np operations fine.

S/R:

```python
def _swing_mask(series: pd.Series, is_high: bool, lookback: int) -> pd.Series:
    """Boolean mask of fractal swing points using a centered window."""
    window = 2 * lookback + 1
    if is_high:
        return series == series.rolling(window, center=True).max()
    return series == series.rolling(window, center=True).min()


def _add_support_resistance(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """Rolling floor pivots + fractal swing highs/lows (both lookahead-safe)."""
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    atr = df["atr"]

    # -- rolling pivot points from the *previous* PIVOT_WINDOW bars -----------
    prev_high = high.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).max().shift(1)
    prev_low = low.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).min().shift(1)
    prev_close = close.shift(1)

    pivot = (prev_high + prev_low + prev_close) / 3.0
    window_range = (prev_high - prev_low).replace(0.0, np.nan)

    df["pivot"] = pivot
    df["dist_pivot_atr"] = (close - pivot) / atr
    df["dist_r1_atr"] = (close - (2.0 * pivot - prev_low)) / atr
    df["dist_s1_atr"] = (close - (2.0 * pivot - prev_high)) / atr
    df["dist_r2_atr"] = (close - (pivot + window_range)) / atr
    df["dist_s2_atr"] = (close - (pivot - window_range)) / atr

    # -- fractal swing highs / lows -------------------------------------------
    # A swing at bar t needs `SWING_LOOKBACK` bars on each side to confirm, so
    # the level is shifted forward by SWING_LOOKBACK bars before forward
    # filling: at any row only *confirmed* swings are visible (no lookahead).
    for side, series, is_high in (("high", high, True), ("low", low, False)):
        mask = _swing_mask(series, is_high, SWING_LOOKBACK)
        confirmed = series.where(mask).shift(SWING_LOOKBACK)
        last_swing = confirmed.ffill()
        df[f"dist_swing_{side}_atr"] = (close - last_swing) / atr
```

Per-timeframe assembly:

```python
def compute_timeframe_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Compute all features for a single (symbol, timeframe) OHLCV DataFrame."""
    features = df.copy()
    _add_core_indicators(features)
    _add_price_volume_features(features)
    _add_support_resistance(features)
    return features
```

Merge:

```python
def merge_higher_timeframe(
    base: pd.DataFrame, higher: pd.DataFrame, higher_tf: str
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Align higher-timeframe features onto the base (5m) index, lookahead-safe.

    A higher-TF bar stamped T only *closes* at T + timeframe, so its features
    are shifted by one bar before being forward-filled onto the base index.
    """
    shifted = higher.shift(1)
    aligned = shifted.reindex(base.index, method="ffill")
    aligned.columns = [f"{higher_tf}_{column}" for column in aligned.columns]
    return pd.concat([base, aligned], axis=1)
```

Build:

```python
def build_feature_matrix(
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
    symbol: str,
    base_timeframe: str = BASE_TIMEFRAME,
    higher_timeframes: Tuple[str, ...] = HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES,
) -> Optional[pd.DataFrame]:
    base_key = (symbol, base_timeframe)
    if base_key not in data:
        logger.warning("No %s data for %s - cannot build features.", base_timeframe, symbol)
        return None
    matrix = compute_timeframe_features(data[base_key])
    for tf in higher_timeframes:
        key = (symbol, tf)
        if key not in data:
            logger.warning("No %s data for %s - skipping higher-TF merge.", tf, symbol)
            continue
        higher = compute_timeframe_features(data[key])
        matrix = merge_higher_timeframe(matrix, higher, tf)
    return matrix


def build_all_feature_matrices(
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    symbols = sorted({symbol for symbol, _ in data})
    matrices: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame] = {}
    for symbol in symbols:
        matrix = build_feature_matrix(data, symbol)
        if matrix is not None:
            matrices[symbol] = matrix
    logger.info("Built feature matrices for %d/%d symbols.", len(matrices), len(symbols))
    return matrices
```

Now sentiment_analyzer.py full code:

```python
"""
sentiment_analyzer.py
=====================

Task 4 - News & sentiment features for the ML scalping engine.

Data sources (in priority order, all key-free friendly):
    1. CryptoPanic free public API - used when the optional
       ``CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN`` environment variable is set (the token is
       free, but the integration works without it).
    2. RSS fallback (CoinDesk / CoinTelegraph / Decrypt / Bitcoin Magazine)
       filtered by asset-specific keywords.
    3. If both fail or return nothing, a neutral score of 0.0 is emitted so
       the ML pipeline never breaks.

Scoring is a transparent keyword lexicon in [-1, +1] (no external NLP model
required); it can later be swapped for a proper sentiment model.

Alignment
---------
Posts are bucketed on a 1-minute grid, then converted into trailing-window
statistics (mean score over the last 2h, post count over the last 1h) and
forward-filled onto the candle index. A post is treated as known from its
publication time onwards - no lookahead in the other direction either.

Usage
-----
    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)          # feature_engineering.py
    matrices = await add_sentiment(matrices)             # adds sentiment columns

Columns added per symbol:
    sentiment_asset    - lexicon score for this asset's news  ([-1, 1])
    sentiment_market   - lexicon score for BTC news (market proxy)
    sentiment_combined - asset score when fresh asset news exists, else market
    news_count_1h      - number of asset posts in the trailing hour

Standalone smoke test (no exchange data required):
    python sentiment_analyzer.py
"""
```

Imports and config:

```python
from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
import os
import re
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple

import feedparser
import pandas as pd
import requests
from dotenv import load_dotenv
from tenacity import (
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_fixed,
)

load_dotenv()  # optional .env support for CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("sentiment_analyzer")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

CRYPTOPANIC_URL = "https://cryptopanic.com/api/free/v1/posts/"
CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV = "CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN"
REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10
CRYPTOPANIC_MAX_PAGES = 2

RSS_FEEDS: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "https://www.coindesk.com/arc/outboundfeeds/rss/",
    "https://cointelegraph.com/rss",
    "https://decrypt.co/feed",
    "https://bitcoinmagazine.com/feed",
)

MARKET_PROXY_ASSET = "BTC"       # market-wide sentiment proxy
SCORE_WINDOW = "2h"              # trailing window for the mean sentiment score
COUNT_WINDOW = "1h"              # trailing window for the post count
POST_GRID = "1min"               # internal binning grid before alignment
CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 300          # per-asset news cache lifetime

# Keyword lexicon (lowercase substrings). Deliberately simple and auditable.
POSITIVE_PHRASES: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "surge", "surges", "soar", "soars", "rally", "rallies", "bullish",
    "jumps", "breakout", "record high", "all-time high", "ath",
    "adoption", "partnership", "upgrade", "approval", "approves",
    "inflow", "inflows", "accumulat", "gain", "gains", "boost", "boosts",
    "beats", "buy the dip", "pump", "green", "rebound", "recovers",
)
NEGATIVE_PHRASES: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "crash", "crashes", "plunge", "plunges", "plummet", "dump", "dumps",
    "bearish", "hack", "hacked", "exploit", "breach", "lawsuit", "sues",
    "sued", "ban", "bans", "banned", "fraud", "ponzi", "scam", "rug pull",
    "liquidation", "liquidated", "sell-off", "selloff", "slump", "tumble",
    "tumbles", "sink", "sinks", "drop", "drops", "fall", "falls", "fear",
    "fud", "warning", "warns", "outflow", "outflows", "delist", "probe",
    "investigation", "charged", "arrest", "downgrade", "loss", "losses",
)

# Keyword aliases used to filter generic RSS feeds per asset.
ASSET_KEYWORDS: Dict[str, Tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "BTC": ("bitcoin", "btc"),
    "ETH": ("ethereum", "ether", "eth"),
    "SOL": ("solana", "sol"),
    "XRP": ("ripple", "xrp"),
    "BNB": ("bnb", "binance coin"),
    "ADA": ("cardano", "ada"),
    "DOGE": ("dogecoin", "doge"),
    "AVAX": ("avalanche", "avax"),
    "DOT": ("polkadot", "dot"),
    "LINK": ("chainlink", "link"),
    "MATIC": ("polygon", "matic"),
    "LTC": ("litecoin", "ltc"),
    "TRX": ("tron", "trx"),
    "SHIB": ("shiba", "shib"),
    "NEAR": ("near protocol", "near"),
    "ATOM": ("cosmos", "atom"),
    "UNI": ("uniswap", "uni"),
    "PEPE": ("pepe",),
```

NewsPost dataclass:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NewsPost:
    published_at: pd.Timestamp
    title: str
    source: str
    currencies: Tuple[str, ...] = ()
```

Scoring:

```python
def score_title(title: str) -> float:
    """
    Lexicon sentiment for a headline in [-1.0, +1.0].

    +1.0 -> only positive phrases, -1.0 -> only negative phrases,
     0.0 -> no lexicon hits (neutral).
    """
    text = (title or "").lower()
    if not text:
        return 0.0
    positive = sum(1 for phrase in POSITIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    negative = sum(1 for phrase in NEGATIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    total = positive + negative
    if total == 0:
        return 0.0
    return (positive - negative) / total
```

CryptoPanic:

```python
@retry(
    retry=retry_if_exception_type((requests.ConnectionError, requests.Timeout)),
    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
    wait=wait_fixed(2),
    reraise=True,
)
def _fetch_cryptopanic(currency: str, auth_token: str) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """Fetch recent posts for one currency from the CryptoPanic free API."""
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    url: Optional[str] = CRYPTOPANIC_URL
    params: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = {
        "auth_token": auth_token,
        "currencies": currency.upper(),
        "public": "true",
    }
    for _ in range(CRYPTOPANIC_MAX_PAGES):
        response = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)
        response.raise_for_status()
        payload = response.json()
        for item in payload.get("results", []):
            published = item.get("published_at")
            if not published:
                continue
            posts.append(
                NewsPost(
                    published_at=pd.to_datetime(published, utc=True),
                    title=item.get("title", ""),
                    source=(item.get("source") or {}).get("title", "cryptopanic"),
                    currencies=tuple(
                        c.get("code", "") for c in item.get("currencies", []) or []
                    ),
                )
            )
        url = payload.get("next")          # CryptoPanic paginates via 'next'
        params = None
        if not url:
            break
    return posts
```

RSS:

```python
def _fetch_rss(keywords: Sequence[str]) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """Filter public crypto RSS feeds for headlines matching `keywords`."""
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    lowered = tuple(keyword.lower() for keyword in keywords)
    for feed_url in RSS_FEEDS:
        try:
            parsed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("RSS feed failed (%s): %s", feed_url, exc)
            continue
        for entry in parsed.entries[:50]:
            title = getattr(entry, "title", "") or ""
            text = title.lower()
            if not text or not any(keyword in text for keyword in lowered):
                continue
            stamp = entry.get("published_parsed") or entry.get("updated_parsed")
            if stamp is None:
                continue  # cannot align a post without a timestamp
            published = pd.Timestamp(datetime(*stamp[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc))
            posts.append(NewsPost(published, title, feed_url, ()))
    return posts
```

Cache + orchestrator:

```python
_posts_cache: Dict[str, Tuple[float, List[NewsPost]]] = {}
_no_token_logged = False


def get_posts_for_asset(base: str, use_cache: bool = True) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """
    Return recent news posts for one base asset (e.g. 'BTC').

    Tries CryptoPanic (if a token is configured), then the RSS fallback.
    Returns an empty list when both are unavailable - callers treat that as
    a neutral sentiment score.
    """
    global _no_token_logged

    key = base.upper()
    now = time.time()
    cached = _posts_cache.get(key)
    if use_cache and cached is not None and now - cached[0] < CACHE_TTL_SECONDS:
        return cached[1]

    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    token = os.getenv(CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV)
    if token:
        try:
            posts = _fetch_cryptopanic(key, token)
            logger.info("CryptoPanic: %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("CryptoPanic failed for %s (%s) - trying RSS.", key, exc)
    elif not _no_token_logged:
        logger.info(
            "No %s set - using free RSS fallback (neutral scores if it fails).",
            CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV,
        )
        _no_token_logged = True

    if not posts:
        keywords = ASSET_KEYWORDS.get(key, (key.lower(),))
        try:
            posts = _fetch_rss(keywords)
            logger.info("RSS fallback: %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("RSS fallback failed for %s: %s", key, exc)
            posts = []

    _posts_cache[key] = (now, posts)
    return posts
```

Alignment:

```python
def sentiment_features(posts: Sequence[NewsPost], index: pd.DatetimeIndex) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Build lookahead-safe sentiment columns aligned to a candle index.

    sentiment_score : trailing mean lexicon score over the last SCORE_WINDOW
    news_count      : number of posts in the trailing COUNT_WINDOW
    Rows before the first post (or with no posts at all) are neutral (0.0).
    """
    empty = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "sentiment_score": pd.Series(0.0, index=index),
            "news_count": pd.Series(0, index=index),
        }
    )
    if not posts or len(index) == 0:
        return empty

    frame = pd.DataFrame(
        {
            "published_at": [post.published_at for post in posts],
            "score": [score_title(post.title) for post in posts],
        }
    ).set_index("published_at").sort_index()

    # Bin posts on a 1-minute grid, then compute trailing-window statistics.
    per_minute = frame["score"].resample(POST_GRID).mean()
    score = per_minute.rolling(SCORE_WINDOW, min_periods=1).mean()
    count = (
        frame["score"]
        .resample(POST_GRID)
        .count()
        .rolling(COUNT_WINDOW, min_periods=1)
        .sum()
    )

    score = score.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0.0)
    count = count.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0).astype(int)

    return pd.DataFrame({"sentiment_score": score, "news_count": count})
```

Wait: `count.reindex(index, method="ffill")` — count index is 1-min grid; candle index 5-min; reindex with ffill works. But if candle index has timestamps not on the 1-min grid (they are), fine.

Edge: `resample` requires datetime index — yes. `rolling('2h')` on the resampled series — the resampled series has regular 1-min frequency; time-based rolling works.

One issue: `score.reindex(index, method="ffill")` — if `index` contains timestamps *before* the first post, those get NaN → fillna(0). Good. If index tz-aware UTC and post grid tz-aware UTC — both UTC, reindex matches. Good.

Attach:

```python
def attach_sentiment(
    features: pd.DataFrame,
    base: str,
    market_posts: Optional[List[NewsPost]] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Merge sentiment columns into one symbol's feature matrix."""
    index = features.index
    asset_posts = get_posts_for_asset(base)

    if market_posts is None:
        if base.upper() == MARKET_PROXY_ASSET:
            market_posts = asset_posts
        else:
            market_posts = get_posts_for_asset(MARKET_PROXY_ASSET)

    asset_sentiment = sentiment_features(asset_posts, index)
    market_sentiment = sentiment_features(market_posts, index)

    out = features.copy()
    out["sentiment_asset"] = asset_sentiment["sentiment_score"]
    out["sentiment_market"] = market_sentiment["sentiment_score"]
    out["news_count_1h"] = asset_sentiment["news_count"]

    # Prefer asset-specific news; fall back to the market proxy when the
    # asset has no fresh coverage.
    out["sentiment_combined"] = out["sentiment_asset"].where(
        out["news_count_1h"] > 0, out["sentiment_market"]
    )
    return out
```

Async:

```python
async def add_sentiment(
    features_by_symbol: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Enrich every symbol's feature matrix with sentiment columns.

    Network calls are blocking (requests/feedparser), so they run in worker
    threads; results are gathered concurrently.
    """

    async def _enrich(symbol: str, features: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
        base = symbol.split("/")[0]
        return await asyncio.to_thread(attach_sentiment, features, base)

    pairs = list(features_by_symbol.items())
    enriched = await asyncio.gather(*(_enrich(symbol, feats) for symbol, feats in pairs))
    return {symbol: matrix for (symbol, _), matrix in zip(pairs, enriched)}
```

Final code for feature_engineering.py:

```python
"""
feature_engineering.py
======================

Task 3 - Feature engineering for the ML scalping engine.

Consumes the tidy OHLCV DataFrames produced by ``data_pipeline.py`` (keys are
``(symbol, timeframe)`` tuples, values are UTC-indexed DataFrames with
``open/high/low/close/volume`` columns) and builds one feature matrix per
symbol, ready for the ML stage.

Feature groups (computed per timeframe)
---------------------------------------
* RSI(14)
* MACD(12, 26, 9): line, signal, histogram
* ATR(14) and ATR as a fraction of price
* EMA(9, 21, 50, 200) plus close-vs-EMA distance in %
* Bollinger Bands(20, 2): lower/mid/upper, bandwidth, %B
* VWAP (daily anchored) plus close-vs-VWAP distance in %
* Multi-horizon returns (1, 3, 5 bars) and realized volatility
* Volume z-score and volume ratio
* Candle anatomy: body / upper wick / lower wick as a fraction of bar range
* Cyclical time-of-day encoding (sin / cos)
* Dynamic support & resistance:
    - Classic floor pivot points (P, R1, S1, R2, S2) computed on a rolling
      *previous* window (shifted, so no lookahead); distances in ATR units.
    - Fractal swing highs/lows; a swing is only surfaced once confirmed by
      SWING_LOOKBACK bars on the right, then forward-filled.

Multi-timeframe alignment (no lookahead)
----------------------------------------
The 15m feature block is aligned onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift and
forward-fill: a 15m bar stamped T only closes at T+15m, so a 5m row inside
that bar sees the *previous* 15m bar's features. Higher-timeframe columns are
prefixed with the timeframe (e.g. ``15m_rsi``).

Usage
-----
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    from feature_engineering import build_all_feature_matrices
    from sentiment_analyzer import add_sentiment

    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)      # {symbol: DataFrame}
    matrices = await add_sentiment(matrices)         # optional Task 4 merge

Standalone smoke test (hits the Binance public API):
    python feature_engineering.py
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pandas_ta as ta

from data_pipeline import load_market_data

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BASE_TIMEFRAME = "5m"
HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("15m",)

EMA_LENGTHS: Tuple[int, ...] = (9, 21, 50, 200)
RSI_LENGTH = 14
MACD_FAST, MACD_SLOW, MACD_SIGNAL = 12, 26, 9
ATR_LENGTH = 14
BB_LENGTH, BB_STD = 20, 2.0
VWAP_ANCHOR = "D"               # daily-anchored VWAP (UTC sessions)

RETURN_LAGS: Tuple[int, ...] = (1, 3, 5)
VOLATILITY_WINDOW = 12          # bars for realized volatility of 1-bar returns
VOLUME_WINDOW = 20              # rolling window for volume z-score / ratio

SWING_LOOKBACK = 3              # bars on each side for fractal swing points
PIVOT_WINDOW = 12               # rolling window for floor pivots (1h on 5m)

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("feature_engineering")
```

```python
def _macd_columns(close: pd.Series) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    MACD line, histogram and signal as a DataFrame.

    pandas-ta 0.3.14b0 returns columns in the order
    [MACD_12_26_9, MACDh_12_26_9, MACDs_12_26_9]; we access them by name and
    fall back to positional access if the naming ever changes.
    """
    macd_df = ta.macd(close, fast=MACD_FAST, slow=MACD_SLOW, signal=MACD_SIGNAL)
    index = close.index
    if macd_df is None or macd_df.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(
            {"macd": np.nan, "macd_signal": np.nan, "macd_hist": np.nan}, index=index
        )

    suffix = f"{MACD_FAST}_{MACD_SLOW}_{MACD_SIGNAL}"
    macd_line = macd_df.get(f"MACD_{suffix}")
    histogram = macd_df.get(f"MACDh_{suffix}")
    signal = macd_df.get(f"MACDs_{suffix}")
    if macd_line is None or histogram is None or signal is None:
        macd_line, histogram, signal = (
            macd_df.iloc[:, 0], macd_df.iloc[:, 1], macd_df.iloc[:, 2],
        )
    return pd.DataFrame(
        {"macd": macd_line, "macd_signal": signal, "macd_hist": histogram},
        index=index,
    )
```

`macd_df.get(col)` — DataFrame.get works with column key, returns default None. Good.

</thinking-content-7bbeb8e1441453ad999a0bbba8a46d4b>

Both new files are designed to plug directly into `BinanceDataPipeline.run()` output (`{(symbol, timeframe): DataFrame}`). Key design decisions:

**`feature_engineering.py` (Task 3)**
- Computes RSI, MACD, ATR, EMAs (9/21/50/200), Bollinger Bands, daily-anchored VWAP, plus returns, realized volatility, volume z-score, candle anatomy and cyclical time-of-day features.
- Dynamic S/R: classic floor pivots (P/R1/S1/R2/S2) from a rolling *previous* window, and fractal swing highs/lows that are only surfaced after confirmation — both lookahead-safe, distances expressed in ATR units.
- The 15m feature block is merged onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift + forward-fill, so a 5m row never sees a 15m bar that hasn't closed yet. Higher-TF columns get a `15m_` prefix.

**`sentiment_analyzer.py` (Task 4)**
- Uses the CryptoPanic free public endpoint when `CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN` is set (env var or `.env`); otherwise falls back to free RSS feeds filtered by per-asset keywords; if both fail it emits a neutral `0.0` so the ML pipeline never breaks.
- Transparent keyword-lexicon scoring in `[-1, +1]` (swappable for a real NLP model later).
- Posts are binned on a 1-minute grid, converted to trailing-window stats (mean score over 2h, post count over 1h) and forward-filled onto the candle index — a post is only known from its publication time onward.
- Adds `sentiment_asset`, `sentiment_market` (BTC proxy), `sentiment_combined`, and `news_count_1h` to each feature matrix.

No changes to `requirements.txt` were needed — `requests`, `feedparser`, `python-dotenv` and `tenacity` are already declared.

feature_engineering.py
```
"""
feature_engineering.py
======================

Task 3 - Feature engineering for the ML scalping engine.

Consumes the tidy OHLCV DataFrames produced by ``data_pipeline.py`` (keys are
``(symbol, timeframe)`` tuples, values are UTC-indexed DataFrames with
``open/high/low/close/volume`` columns) and builds one feature matrix per
symbol, ready for the ML stage.

Feature groups (computed per timeframe)
---------------------------------------
* RSI(14)
* MACD(12, 26, 9): line, signal, histogram
* ATR(14) and ATR as a fraction of price
* EMA(9, 21, 50, 200) plus close-vs-EMA distance in %
* Bollinger Bands(20, 2): lower/mid/upper, bandwidth, %B
* VWAP (daily anchored) plus close-vs-VWAP distance in %
* Multi-horizon returns (1, 3, 5 bars) and realized volatility
* Volume z-score and volume ratio
* Candle anatomy: body / upper wick / lower wick as a fraction of bar range
* Cyclical time-of-day encoding (sin / cos)
* Dynamic support & resistance:
    - Classic floor pivot points (P, R1, S1, R2, S2) computed on a rolling
      *previous* window (shifted, so no lookahead); distances in ATR units.
    - Fractal swing highs/lows; a swing is only surfaced once confirmed by
      SWING_LOOKBACK bars on the right, then forward-filled.

Multi-timeframe alignment (no lookahead)
----------------------------------------
The 15m feature block is aligned onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift and
forward-fill: a 15m bar stamped T only closes at T+15m, so a 5m row inside
that bar sees the *previous* 15m bar's features. Higher-timeframe columns are
prefixed with the timeframe (e.g. ``15m_rsi``).

Usage
-----
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    from feature_engineering import build_all_feature_matrices
    from sentiment_analyzer import add_sentiment

    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)      # {symbol: DataFrame}
    matrices = await add_sentiment(matrices)         # optional Task 4 merge

Standalone smoke test (hits the Binance public API):
```
