```
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pandas_ta as ta

from data_pipeline import load_market_data

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

BASE_TIMEFRAME = "5m"
HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES: Tuple[str, ...] = ("15m",)

EMA_LENGTHS: Tuple[int, ...] = (9, 21, 50, 200)
RSI_LENGTH = 14
MACD_FAST, MACD_SLOW, MACD_SIGNAL = 12, 26, 9
ATR_LENGTH = 14
BB_LENGTH, BB_STD = 20, 2.0
VWAP_ANCHOR = "D"               # daily-anchored VWAP (UTC sessions)

RETURN_LAGS: Tuple[int, ...] = (1, 3, 5)
VOLATILITY_WINDOW = 12          # bars for realized volatility of 1-bar returns
VOLUME_WINDOW = 20              # rolling window for volume z-score / ratio

SWING_LOOKBACK = 3              # bars on each side for fractal swing points
PIVOT_WINDOW = 12               # rolling window for floor pivots (1h on 5m)

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("feature_engineering")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Indicator helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _macd_columns(close: pd.Series) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    MACD line, histogram and signal as a DataFrame.

    pandas-ta 0.3.14b0 returns columns in the order
    [MACD_12_26_9, MACDh_12_26_9, MACDs_12_26_9]; we access them by name and
    fall back to positional access if the naming ever changes.
    """
    macd_df = ta.macd(close, fast=MACD_FAST, slow=MACD_SLOW, signal=MACD_SIGNAL)
    index = close.index
    if macd_df is None or macd_df.empty:
        return pd.DataFrame(
            {"macd": np.nan, "macd_signal": np.nan, "macd_hist": np.nan},
            index=index,
        )

    suffix = f"{MACD_FAST}_{MACD_SLOW}_{MACD_SIGNAL}"
    macd_line = macd_df.get(f"MACD_{suffix}")
    histogram = macd_df.get(f"MACDh_{suffix}")
    signal = macd_df.get(f"MACDs_{suffix}")
    if macd_line is None or histogram is None or signal is None:
        macd_line = macd_df.iloc[:, 0]
        histogram = macd_df.iloc[:, 1]
        signal = macd_df.iloc[:, 2]

    return pd.DataFrame(
        {"macd": macd_line, "macd_signal": signal, "macd_hist": histogram},
        index=index,
    )


def _add_core_indicators(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """Add RSI, MACD, ATR, EMAs, Bollinger Bands and VWAP (in place)."""
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    volume = df["volume"]

    # -- momentum -------------------------------------------------------------
    rsi = ta.rsi(close, length=RSI_LENGTH)
    df["rsi"] = rsi if rsi is not None else np.nan

    macd = _macd_columns(close)
    df["macd"] = macd["macd"]
    df["macd_signal"] = macd["macd_signal"]
    df["macd_hist"] = macd["macd_hist"]

    # -- volatility -------------------------------------------------------------
    atr = ta.atr(high, low, close, length=ATR_LENGTH)
    df["atr"] = atr if atr is not None else np.nan
    df["atr_pct"] = df["atr"] / close

    bb = ta.bbands(close, length=BB_LENGTH, std=BB_STD)
    if bb is not None and not bb.empty:
        # pandas-ta 0.3.14b0 column order: BBL, BBM, BBU, BBB (bandwidth), BBP (%B)
        df["bb_lower"] = bb.iloc[:, 0]
        df["bb_mid"] = bb.iloc[:, 1]
        df["bb_upper"] = bb.iloc[:, 2]
        df["bb_bandwidth"] = bb.iloc[:, 3]
        df["bb_percent"] = bb.iloc[:, 4]
    else:
        for column in ("bb_lower", "bb_mid", "bb_upper", "bb_bandwidth", "bb_percent"):
            df[column] = np.nan

    # -- trend --------------------------------------------------------------------
    for length in EMA_LENGTHS:
        ema = ta.ema(close, length=length)
        if ema is not None:
            df[f"ema_{length}"] = ema
            df[f"close_ema_{length}_pct"] = close / ema - 1.0
        else:
            df[f"ema_{length}"] = np.nan
            df[f"close_ema_{length}_pct"] = np.nan

    # -- VWAP (requires a DatetimeIndex, which the pipeline guarantees) -----------
    vwap = None
    try:
        vwap = ta.vwap(high, low, close, volume, anchor=VWAP_ANCHOR)
    except Exception as exc:  # defensive: never let one indicator kill the run
        logger.warning("VWAP computation failed: %s", exc)
    if vwap is not None:
        df["vwap"] = vwap
        df["close_vwap_pct"] = close / vwap - 1.0
    else:
        df["vwap"] = np.nan
        df["close_vwap_pct"] = np.nan


def _add_price_volume_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """Add returns, volatility, volume and candle-anatomy features (in place)."""
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    open_, volume = df["open"], df["volume"]

    for lag in RETURN_LAGS:
        df[f"ret_{lag}"] = close.pct_change(lag)
    df["realized_vol"] = df["ret_1"].rolling(VOLATILITY_WINDOW).std()

    vol_mean = volume.rolling(VOLUME_WINDOW).mean()
    vol_std = volume.rolling(VOLUME_WINDOW).std()
    df["volume_zscore"] = (volume - vol_mean) / vol_std.replace(0.0, np.nan)
    df["volume_ratio"] = volume / vol_mean.replace(0.0, np.nan)

    bar_range = (high - low).replace(0.0, np.nan)
    df["body_pct"] = (close - open_) / bar_range
    df["upper_wick_pct"] = (high - df[["open", "close"]].max(axis=1)) / bar_range
    df["lower_wick_pct"] = (df[["open", "close"]].min(axis=1) - low) / bar_range

    # Cyclical time-of-day encoding (scalping activity is session dependent).
    minutes = df.index.hour * 60 + df.index.minute
    angle = 2.0 * np.pi * minutes / 1440.0
    df["tod_sin"] = np.sin(angle)
    df["tod_cos"] = np.cos(angle)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dynamic support / resistance
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _swing_mask(series: pd.Series, is_high: bool, lookback: int) -> pd.Series:
    """Boolean mask of fractal swing points using a centered window."""
    window = 2 * lookback + 1
    if is_high:
        return series == series.rolling(window, center=True).max()
    return series == series.rolling(window, center=True).min()


def _add_support_resistance(df: pd.DataFrame) -> None:
    """Rolling floor pivots + fractal swing highs/lows (both lookahead-safe)."""
    close, high, low = df["close"], df["high"], df["low"]
    atr = df["atr"]

    # -- rolling pivot points from the *previous* PIVOT_WINDOW bars --------------
    prev_high = high.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).max().shift(1)
    prev_low = low.rolling(PIVOT_WINDOW).min().shift(1)
    prev_close = close.shift(1)

    pivot = (prev_high + prev_low + prev_close) / 3.0
    window_range = (prev_high - prev_low).replace(0.0, np.nan)

    df["pivot"] = pivot
    df["dist_pivot_atr"] = (close - pivot) / atr
    df["dist_r1_atr"] = (close - (2.0 * pivot - prev_low)) / atr
    df["dist_s1_atr"] = (close - (2.0 * pivot - prev_high)) / atr
    df["dist_r2_atr"] = (close - (pivot + window_range)) / atr
    df["dist_s2_atr"] = (close - (pivot - window_range)) / atr

    # -- fractal swing highs / lows ------------------------------------------------
    # A swing at bar t needs SWING_LOOKBACK bars on each side to confirm, so the
    # level is shifted forward by SWING_LOOKBACK bars before forward filling:
    # at any row only *confirmed* swings are visible (no lookahead).
    for side, series, is_high in (("high", high, True), ("low", low, False)):
        mask = _swing_mask(series, is_high, SWING_LOOKBACK)
        confirmed = series.where(mask).shift(SWING_LOOKBACK)
        last_swing = confirmed.ffill()
        df[f"dist_swing_{side}_atr"] = (close - last_swing) / atr


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Assembly
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def compute_timeframe_features(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Compute all features for a single (symbol, timeframe) OHLCV DataFrame."""
    features = df.copy()
    _add_core_indicators(features)
    _add_price_volume_features(features)
    _add_support_resistance(features)
    return features


def merge_higher_timeframe(
    base: pd.DataFrame, higher: pd.DataFrame, higher_tf: str
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Align higher-timeframe features onto the base (5m) index, lookahead-safe.

    A higher-TF bar stamped T only *closes* at T + timeframe, so its features
    are shifted by one bar before being forward-filled onto the base index.
    """
    shifted = higher.shift(1)
    aligned = shifted.reindex(base.index, method="ffill")
    aligned.columns = [f"{higher_tf}_{column}" for column in aligned.columns]
    return pd.concat([base, aligned], axis=1)


def build_feature_matrix(
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
    symbol: str,
    base_timeframe: str = BASE_TIMEFRAME,
    higher_timeframes: Tuple[str, ...] = HIGHER_TIMEFRAMES,
    dropna: bool = False,
) -> Optional[pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Build the full feature matrix for one symbol.

    Parameters
    ----------
    data : output of ``BinanceDataPipeline.run()``
    symbol : e.g. ``"BTC/USDT"``
    dropna : optionally drop warm-up rows containing NaN features
    """
    base_key = (symbol, base_timeframe)
    if base_key not in data:
        logger.warning("No %s data for %s - cannot build features.", base_timeframe, symbol)
        return None

    matrix = compute_timeframe_features(data[base_key])

    for timeframe in higher_timeframes:
        key = (symbol, timeframe)
        if key not in data:
            logger.warning("No %s data for %s - skipping higher-TF merge.", timeframe, symbol)
            continue
        higher = compute_timeframe_features(data[key])
        matrix = merge_higher_timeframe(matrix, higher, timeframe)

    if dropna:
        matrix = matrix.dropna()
    return matrix


def build_all_feature_matrices(
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    """Build feature matrices for every symbol present in ``data``."""
    symbols = sorted({symbol for symbol, _ in data})
    matrices: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame] = {}
    for symbol in symbols:
        matrix = build_feature_matrix(data, symbol)
        if matrix is not None:
            matrices[symbol] = matrix
    logger.info("Built feature matrices for %d/%d symbols.", len(matrices), len(symbols))
    return matrices


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Smoke test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

async def _main() -> None:
    """Smoke test: download a small universe and print feature summaries."""
    symbols, data = await load_market_data(top_n=3)
    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)

    preview_columns = [
        "close", "rsi", "macd_hist", "atr_pct", "bb_percent", "close_vwap_pct",
        "ret_1", "volume_zscore", "dist_pivot_atr", "dist_swing_high_atr",
        "dist_swing_low_atr", "15m_rsi", "15m_atr_pct",
    ]
    for symbol, matrix in matrices.items():
        print("\n" + "=" * 78)
        print(f"{symbol}: {matrix.shape[0]} rows x {matrix.shape[1]} features")
        print("=" * 78)
        available = [column for column in preview_columns if column in matrix.columns]
        for column, value in matrix[available].iloc[-1].items():
            print(f"  {column:<22} {value: .6f}")
        nan_share = matrix.isna().mean().mean()
        print(f"  {'<NaN share>':<22} {nan_share: .2%}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted by user.")
```

sentiment_analyzer.py
```
"""
sentiment_analyzer.py
=====================

Task 4 - News & sentiment features for the ML scalping engine.

Data sources (in priority order, all key-free friendly):
    1. CryptoPanic free public API - used when the optional
       ``CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN`` environment variable is set (the token is
       free, but the integration also works without it).
    2. RSS fallback (CoinDesk / CoinTelegraph / Decrypt / Bitcoin Magazine)
       filtered by asset-specific keywords.
    3. If both fail or return nothing, a neutral score of 0.0 is emitted so
       the ML pipeline never breaks.

Scoring is a transparent keyword lexicon in [-1, +1] (no external NLP model
required); it can later be swapped for a proper sentiment model without
touching the alignment logic.

Alignment (no lookahead)
------------------------
Posts are binned on a 1-minute grid, converted into trailing-window statistics
(mean score over the last 2h, post count over the last 1h) and forward-filled
onto the candle index. A post is treated as known from its publication time
onwards - never before.

Usage
-----
    from feature_engineering import build_all_feature_matrices
    from sentiment_analyzer import add_sentiment

    matrices = build_all_feature_matrices(data)   # {symbol: DataFrame}
    matrices = await add_sentiment(matrices)      # adds sentiment columns

Columns added per symbol:
    sentiment_asset    - lexicon score for this asset's news ([-1, 1])
    sentiment_market   - lexicon score for BTC news (market-wide proxy)
    sentiment_combined - asset score when fresh asset news exists, else market
    news_count_1h      - number of asset posts in the trailing hour

Standalone smoke test (no exchange data required):
    python sentiment_analyzer.py
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
import os
import time
from dataclasses import dataclass
from datetime import datetime, timezone
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Sequence, Tuple

import feedparser
import pandas as pd
import requests
from dotenv import load_dotenv
from tenacity import (
    retry,
    retry_if_exception_type,
    stop_after_attempt,
    wait_fixed,
)

load_dotenv()  # optional .env support for CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("sentiment_analyzer")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

CRYPTOPANIC_URL = "https://cryptopanic.com/api/free/v1/posts/"
CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV = "CRYPTOPANIC_AUTH_TOKEN"
REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 10
CRYPTOPANIC_MAX_PAGES = 2

RSS_FEEDS: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "https://www.coindesk.com/arc/outboundfeeds/rss/",
    "https://cointelegraph.com/rss",
    "https://decrypt.co/feed",
    "https://bitcoinmagazine.com/feed",
)

MARKET_PROXY_ASSET = "BTC"       # market-wide sentiment proxy
SCORE_WINDOW = "2h"              # trailing window for the mean sentiment score
COUNT_WINDOW = "1h"              # trailing window for the post count
POST_GRID = "1min"               # internal binning grid before alignment
CACHE_TTL_SECONDS = 300          # per-asset news cache lifetime
SENTIMENT_CONCURRENCY = 5        # parallel asset fetches

# Keyword lexicon (lowercase substrings). Deliberately simple and auditable.
POSITIVE_PHRASES: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "surge", "surges", "soar", "soars", "rally", "rallies", "bullish",
    "jumps", "breakout", "record high", "all-time high", "ath",
    "adoption", "partnership", "upgrade", "approval", "approves",
    "inflow", "inflows", "accumulat", "gain", "gains", "boost", "boosts",
    "beats", "buy the dip", "pump", "rebound", "recovers",
)
NEGATIVE_PHRASES: Tuple[str, ...] = (
    "crash", "crashes", "plunge", "plunges", "plummet", "dump", "dumps",
    "bearish", "hack", "hacked", "exploit", "breach", "lawsuit", "sues",
    "sued", "ban", "bans", "banned", "fraud", "ponzi", "scam", "rug pull",
    "liquidation", "liquidated", "sell-off", "selloff", "slump", "tumble",
    "tumbles", "sink", "sinks", "drop", "drops", "fall", "falls", "fear",
    "fud", "warning", "warns", "outflow", "outflows", "delist", "probe",
    "investigation", "charged", "arrest", "downgrade", "loss", "losses",
)

# Keyword aliases used to filter generic RSS feeds per base asset.
ASSET_KEYWORDS: Dict[str, Tuple[str, ...]] = {
    "BTC": ("bitcoin", "btc"),
    "ETH": ("ethereum", "ether", "eth"),
    "SOL": ("solana", "sol"),
    "XRP": ("ripple", "xrp"),
    "BNB": ("bnb", "binance coin"),
    "ADA": ("cardano", "ada"),
    "DOGE": ("dogecoin", "doge"),
    "AVAX": ("avalanche", "avax"),
    "DOT": ("polkadot", "dot"),
    "LINK": ("chainlink", "link"),
    "MATIC": ("polygon", "matic"),
    "LTC": ("litecoin", "ltc"),
    "TRX": ("tron", "trx"),
    "SHIB": ("shiba", "shib"),
    "NEAR": ("near protocol", "near"),
    "ATOM": ("cosmos", "atom"),
    "UNI": ("uniswap",),
    "PEPE": ("pepe",),
}


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Data model
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NewsPost:
    published_at: pd.Timestamp
    title: str
    source: str
    currencies: Tuple[str, ...] = ()


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Scoring
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def score_title(title: str) -> float:
    """
    Lexicon sentiment for a headline in [-1.0, +1.0].

    +1.0 -> only positive phrases, -1.0 -> only negative phrases,
     0.0 -> no lexicon hits (neutral).
    """
    text = (title or "").lower()
    if not text:
        return 0.0
    positive = sum(1 for phrase in POSITIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    negative = sum(1 for phrase in NEGATIVE_PHRASES if phrase in text)
    total = positive + negative
    if total == 0:
        return 0.0
    return (positive - negative) / total


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Fetchers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

@retry(
    retry=retry_if_exception_type((requests.ConnectionError, requests.Timeout)),
    stop=stop_after_attempt(3),
    wait=wait_fixed(2),
    reraise=True,
)
def _fetch_cryptopanic(currency: str, auth_token: str) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """Fetch recent posts for one currency from the CryptoPanic free API."""
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    url: Optional[str] = CRYPTOPANIC_URL
    params: Optional[Dict[str, str]] = {
        "auth_token": auth_token,
        "currencies": currency.upper(),
        "public": "true",
    }
    for _ in range(CRYPTOPANIC_MAX_PAGES):
        response = requests.get(url, params=params, timeout=REQUEST_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)
        response.raise_for_status()
        payload = response.json()
        for item in payload.get("results", []):
            published = item.get("published_at")
            if not published:
                continue
            posts.append(
                NewsPost(
                    published_at=pd.to_datetime(published, utc=True),
                    title=item.get("title", ""),
                    source=(item.get("source") or {}).get("title", "cryptopanic"),
                    currencies=tuple(
                        entry.get("code", "")
                        for entry in item.get("currencies", []) or []
                    ),
                )
            )
        url = payload.get("next")  # CryptoPanic paginates via the 'next' URL
        params = None
        if not url:
            break
    return posts


def _fetch_rss(keywords: Sequence[str]) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """Filter public crypto RSS feeds for headlines matching ``keywords``."""
    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    lowered = tuple(keyword.lower() for keyword in keywords)
    for feed_url in RSS_FEEDS:
        try:
            parsed = feedparser.parse(feed_url)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("RSS feed failed (%s): %s", feed_url, exc)
            continue
        for entry in parsed.entries[:50]:
            title = getattr(entry, "title", "") or ""
            text = title.lower()
            if not text or not any(keyword in text for keyword in lowered):
                continue
            stamp = entry.get("published_parsed") or entry.get("updated_parsed")
            if stamp is None:
                continue  # cannot align a post without a timestamp
            published = pd.Timestamp(datetime(*stamp[:6], tzinfo=timezone.utc))
            posts.append(NewsPost(published, title, feed_url, ()))
    return posts


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Cached per-asset orchestration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

_posts_cache: Dict[str, Tuple[float, List[NewsPost]]] = {}
_no_token_logged = False


def get_posts_for_asset(base: str, use_cache: bool = True) -> List[NewsPost]:
    """
    Return recent news posts for one base asset (e.g. ``"BTC"``).

    Tries CryptoPanic (if a token is configured), then the RSS fallback.
    Returns an empty list when both are unavailable - callers treat that as
    a neutral sentiment score.
    """
    global _no_token_logged

    key = base.upper()
    now = time.time()
    cached = _posts_cache.get(key)
    if use_cache and cached is not None and now - cached[0] < CACHE_TTL_SECONDS:
        return cached[1]

    posts: List[NewsPost] = []
    token = os.getenv(CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV)
    if token:
        try:
            posts = _fetch_cryptopanic(key, token)
            logger.info("CryptoPanic: %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("CryptoPanic failed for %s (%s) - trying RSS.", key, exc)
    elif not _no_token_logged:
        logger.info(
            "No %s set - using free RSS fallback (neutral scores if it fails).",
            CRYPTOPANIC_TOKEN_ENV,
        )
        _no_token_logged = True

    if not posts:
        keywords = ASSET_KEYWORDS.get(key, (key.lower(),))
        try:
            posts = _fetch_rss(keywords)
            logger.info("RSS fallback: %d posts for %s", len(posts), key)
        except Exception as exc:
            logger.warning("RSS fallback failed for %s: %s", key, exc)
            posts = []

    _posts_cache[key] = (now, posts)
    return posts


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Alignment onto the feature index
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def sentiment_features(
    posts: Sequence[NewsPost], index: pd.DatetimeIndex
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Build lookahead-safe sentiment columns aligned to a candle index.

    sentiment_score : trailing mean lexicon score over the last SCORE_WINDOW
    news_count      : number of posts in the trailing COUNT_WINDOW
    Rows before the first post (or with no posts at all) are neutral (0.0).
    """
    if not posts or len(index) == 0:
        return pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "sentiment_score": pd.Series(0.0, index=index),
                "news_count": pd.Series(0, index=index),
            }
        )

    frame = (
        pd.DataFrame(
            {
                "published_at": [post.published_at for post in posts],
                "score": [score_title(post.title) for post in posts],
            }
        )
        .set_index("published_at")
        .sort_index()
    )

    # Bin posts on a 1-minute grid, then compute trailing-window statistics.
    per_minute = frame["score"].resample(POST_GRID).mean()
    score = per_minute.rolling(SCORE_WINDOW, min_periods=1).mean()
    count = (
        frame["score"]
        .resample(POST_GRID)
        .count()
        .rolling(COUNT_WINDOW, min_periods=1)
        .sum()
    )

    score = score.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0.0)
    count = count.reindex(index, method="ffill").fillna(0).astype(int)

    return pd.DataFrame({"sentiment_score": score, "news_count": count})


def attach_sentiment(
    features: pd.DataFrame,
    base: str,
    market_posts: Optional[List[NewsPost]] = None,
) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Merge sentiment columns into one symbol's feature matrix."""
    if not isinstance(features.index, pd.DatetimeIndex):
        raise TypeError("Feature matrix must be indexed by a UTC DatetimeIndex.")

    index = features.index
    asset_posts = get_posts_for_asset(base)

    if market_posts is None:
        if base.upper() == MARKET_PROXY_ASSET:
            market_posts = asset_posts
        else:
            market_posts = get_posts_for_asset(MARKET_PROXY_ASSET)

    asset_sentiment = sentiment_features(asset_posts, index)
    market_sentiment = sentiment_features(market_posts, index)

    out = features.copy()
    out["sentiment_asset"] = asset_sentiment["sentiment_score"]
    out["sentiment_market"] = market_sentiment["sentiment_score"]
    out["news_count_1h"] = asset_sentiment["news_count"]

    # Prefer asset-specific news; fall back to the market proxy when the
    # asset has no fresh coverage.
    out["sentiment_combined"] = out["sentiment_asset"].where(
        out["news_count_1h"] > 0, out["sentiment_market"]
    )
    return out


async def add_sentiment(
    features_by_symbol: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Enrich every symbol's feature matrix with sentiment columns.

    Network calls are blocking (requests/feedparser), so they run in worker
    threads, throttled by a semaphore; results are gathered concurrently.
    """
    semaphore = asyncio.Semaphore(SENTIMENT_CONCURRENCY)

    async def _enrich(symbol: str, features: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
        base = symbol.split("/")[0]
        async with semaphore:
            return await asyncio.to_thread(attach_sentiment, features, base)

    pairs = list(features_by_symbol.items())
    enriched = await asyncio.gather(
        *(_enrich(symbol, features) for symbol, features in pairs)
    )
    return {
        symbol: matrix for (symbol, _), matrix in zip(pairs, enriched)
    }


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Smoke test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

async def _main() -> None:
    """Smoke test with a synthetic 5m index (no exchange data needed)."""
    index = pd.date_range(end=pd.Timestamp.now(tz="UTC"), periods=288, freq="5min")
    demo_symbols = ("BTC/USDT", "ETH/USDT", "SOL/USDT")
    matrices = {symbol: pd.DataFrame(index=index) for symbol in demo_symbols}

    enriched = await add_sentiment(matrices)

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print("SENTIMENT SMOKE TEST (last 3 rows per symbol)")
    print("=" * 78)
    columns = [
```

Helper `_rename_by_prefix`:

```python
def _rename_by_prefix(df: pd.DataFrame, mapping: Dict[str, str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    renamed = {}
    for col in df.columns:
        for prefix, name in mapping.items():
            if col.startswith(prefix):
                renamed[col] = name
                break
    return df.rename(columns=renamed)
```

`add_indicators(df)`:

```python
def add_indicators(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    out = df.copy()
    close, high, low, volume = out["close"], out["high"], out["low"], out["volume"]

    # --- returns / momentum ---
    for h in RETURN_HORIZONS:
        out[f"ret_{h}"] = np.log(close / close.shift(h))
    out["roc_10"] = ta.roc(close, length=10) / 100.0  # ta.roc returns percent
```

Hmm, ta.roc returns percent values. Dividing by 100 normalizes. Fine, or skip roc since we have returns. I'll include it normalized.

```python
    # --- trend ---
    out["ema_fast"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_FAST)
    out["ema_slow"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_SLOW)
    out["ema_trend"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_TREND)
    atr = ta.atr(high, low, close, length=ATR_LEN)
    out["atr"] = atr
    out["atr_pct"] = atr / close
    # distance of price to EMAs, normalized by ATR (scale-free)
    out["close_to_ema_fast"] = (close - out["ema_fast"]) / atr
    out["close_to_ema_slow"] = (close - out["ema_slow"]) / atr
    out["ema_fast_slope"] = out["ema_fast"].diff(3) / atr
    # MACD
    macd = ta.macd(close, fast=MACD_FAST, slow=MACD_SLOW, signal=MACD_SIGNAL)
    macd = _rename_by_prefix(macd, {"MACDh": "macd_hist", "MACDs": "macd_signal", "MACD": "macd"})
    out["macd"] = macd["macd"] / close
    out["macd_signal"] = macd["macd_signal"] / close
    out["macd_hist"] = macd["macd_hist"] / close
    # ADX / DMI
    adx = ta.adx(high, low, close, length=ADX_LEN)
    adx = _rename_by_prefix(adx, {"ADX": "adx", "DMP": "di_plus", "DMN": "di_minus"})
    out["adx"] = adx["adx"]
    out["di_plus"] = adx["di_plus"]
    out["di_minus"] = adx["di_minus"]
```

```python
    # distance to recent extremes (breakout context)
    out["dist_to_high_24"] = (close - high.rolling(ROLL_HIGHLOW_WIN).max()) / atr  # <= 0
    out["dist_to_low_24"] = (close - low.rolling(ROLL_HIGHLOW_WIN).min()) / atr    # >= 0
```

Hmm dist_to_high: close - rolling max of high (including current bar) → ≤ 0, in ATR units. Fine.

Return `out`.

`add_htf_context(df_5m, df_15m)`:

```python
def add_htf_context(df_5m: pd.DataFrame, df_15m: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    out = df_5m.copy()
    h = df_15m.copy()
    h_close, h_high, h_low, h_vol = h["close"], h["high"], h["low"], h["volume"]
    h_atr = ta.atr(h_high, h_low, h_close, length=ATR_LEN)

    ctx = pd.DataFrame(index=h.index)
    ctx["htf_ret_1"] = np.log(h_close / h_close.shift(1))
    ctx["htf_rsi"] = ta.rsi(h_close, length=RSI_LEN)
    ctx["htf_atr_pct"] = h_atr / h_close
    h_ema = ta.ema(h_close, length=EMA_SLOW)
    ctx["htf_trend"] = (h_close - h_ema) / h_atr
    ctx["htf_vol_z"] = (h_vol - h_vol.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).mean()) / h_vol.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).std()

    # Lookahead guard: a 15m bar stamped T only *closes* at T+15m, so shift the
    # whole context one 15m bar into the future before aligning. After the
    # shift, value at index T is the context of the last 15m bar that fully
    # closed before T.
    ctx = ctx.shift(1)
    ctx = ctx.reindex(out.index.union(ctx.index)).ffill().reindex(out.index)
```

Hmm, reindex with union then ffill then reindex to 5m index — simpler: `ctx = ctx.shift(1).reindex(out.index, method="ffill")`. reindex with method='ffill' works if the new index values... Actually `reindex(index, method='ffill')` fills forward for missing labels — requires monotonic index; both are monotonic. But if a 5m timestamp is earlier than the first 15m timestamp, it stays NaN — fine (warmup). Use that simpler form.

```python
    ctx = ctx.shift(1).reindex(out.index, method="ffill")
    for col in ctx.columns:
        out[col] = ctx[col]
    return out
```

```python
def build_features(symbols, data, dropna=True):
    features = {}
    for symbol in symbols:
        df_5m = data.get((symbol, PRIMARY_TF))
        df_15m = data.get((symbol, HTF_TF))
        if df_5m is None:
            logger.warning("%s: no %s data, skipping", symbol, PRIMARY_TF)
            continue
        feat = add_indicators(df_5m)
        if df_15m is not None:
            feat = add_htf_context(feat, df_15m)
        else:
            logger.warning("%s: no %s data, HTF context skipped", symbol, HTF_TF)
        if dropna:
            before = len(feat)
            feat = feat.dropna()
            logger.debug(...)
        features[symbol] = feat
        logger.info("%s: %d feature rows x %d features", symbol, len(feat), feat.shape[1])
    if not features:
        raise RuntimeError("No features could be built - check pipeline output.")
    return features
```

```python
MODEL_DIR = "models"
MODEL_FILENAME = "scalp_model.joblib"

# --- labeling (triple-barrier) ---
PT_ATR_MULT = 1.0     # profit-take barrier: +1.0 x ATR above entry
SL_ATR_MULT = 1.0     # stop-loss barrier:  -1.0 x ATR below entry
LABEL_HORIZON = 6     # time barrier: 6 x 5m = 30 minutes

# --- training ---
TEST_FRACTION = 0.2          # last 20% of the pooled timeline = test set
N_OPTUNA_TRIALS = 25
CV_SPLITS = 3
RANDOM_STATE = 42
```

`triple_barrier_labels(ohlcv, atr, ...)`:

```python
def triple_barrier_labels(ohlcv, atr, pt_mult=PT_ATR_MULT, sl_mult=SL_ATR_MULT, horizon=LABEL_HORIZON):
    """
    Vectorised-ish triple-barrier labeling on 5m bars.

    For each bar t (entry at close[t]):
        upper[t] = close[t] + pt_mult * atr[t]
        lower[t] = close[t] - sl_mult * atr[t]
        scan bars t+1 .. t+horizon:
            high >= upper first  -> 1  (profit-take)
            low  <= lower first  -> 0  (stop-loss)
            neither within horizon -> 1 if close[t+horizon] > close[t] else 0
    Bars without a full forward window or with NaN ATR get NaN labels.
    """
    n = len(ohlcv)
    labels = np.full(n, np.nan)
    close = ohlcv["close"].to_numpy()
    high = ohlcv["high"].to_numpy()
    low = ohlcv["low"].to_numpy()
    atr = atr.to_numpy()

    for t in range(n - horizon):
        a = atr[t]
        if np.isnan(a) or a <= 0:
            continue
        upper = close[t] + pt_mult * a
        lower = close[t] - sl_mult * a
        label = np.nan
        for k in range(1, horizon + 1):
            if high[t + k] >= upper:
                label = 1.0
                break
            if low[t + k] <= lower:
                label = 0.0
                break
        if np.isnan(label):
            label = 1.0 if close[t + horizon] > close[t] else 0.0
        labels[t] = label
    return pd.Series(labels, index=ohlcv.index, name="label")
```

Note: within the same bar k, if both high>=upper and low<=lower, we label 1 (optimistic). Could check which is closer; keep simple, mention in comment. Actually let me handle the ambiguous case: if both hit in same bar, decide by which barrier is closer to open of that bar? Simpler: treat as stop (conservative). I'll add: if both conditions true in same bar → conservative 0. Implement:

```python
        for k in range(1, horizon + 1):
            hit_up = high[t + k] >= upper
            hit_dn = low[t + k] <= lower
            if hit_up and hit_dn:
                label = 0.0  # both barriers in one bar: assume worst case
                break
            if hit_up:
                label = 1.0; break
            if hit_dn:
                label = 0.0; break
```

Good, conservative.

`assemble_dataset(symbols, data, features)`:

```python
def assemble_dataset(symbols, data, features):
    frames = []
    for symbol in symbols:
        feat = features.get(symbol)
        ohlcv = data.get((symbol, PRIMARY_TF))
        if feat is None or ohlcv is None or feat.empty:
            continue
        labels = triple_barrier_labels(ohlcv, feat["atr"])
        df = feat.copy()
        df["label"] = labels
        df["symbol"] = symbol
        frames.append(df)
    if not frames:
        raise RuntimeError(...)
    pooled = pd.concat(frames).sort_index()
    # global chronological split key: use timestamp; but pooling across symbols
    # and sorting by timestamp mixes symbols — that's fine for pooled training,
    # the split below is still strictly time-based.
    pooled = pooled.dropna(subset=["label"])
    y = pooled["label"].astype(int)
    X = pooled[feature_columns(pooled)]  # wait, feature_columns excludes RAW_COLUMNS; "label"/"symbol" not in RAW_COLUMNS so they'd be included!
```


Chronological split: since pooled sorted by timestamp, split by position:

```python
def chronological_split(X, y, test_fraction=TEST_FRACTION):
    split = int(len(X) * (1 - test_fraction))
    return X.iloc[:split], X.iloc[split:], y.iloc[:split], y.iloc[split:]
```

But wait — pooled sorted by timestamp: rows at the same timestamp across symbols are adjacent; the boundary cut is still time-ordered. Good. However there's subtlety: labels use future info; a train row near the boundary has labels extending into test period — minor leakage of label overlap. Standard practice accepts this or adds an embargo. I'll add a small embargo: drop the last LABEL_HORIZON rows of train? Actually embargo means exclude train samples whose label window overlaps test period: drop last `LABEL_HORIZON` timestamps from train. Simple: `split = int(len(X)*(1-test_fraction)) - LABEL_HORIZON`? That drops LABEL_HORIZON rows (across symbols at those timestamps). I'll implement embargo by dropping the final LABEL_HORIZON *distinct timestamps*... simpler: just subtract LABEL_HORIZON rows per symbol? Keep it simple: subtract `LABEL_HORIZON` rows and note it's a simple embargo. Hmm, rows at boundary are grouped by timestamp across ~20 symbols; dropping LABEL_HORIZON rows only drops ~1/20 of a bar's worth. To be cleaner: compute unique timestamps, embargo by timestamps:

```python
split_pos = int(len(X) * (1 - test_fraction))
embargo_ts = X.index.unique().sort_values()[-LABEL_HORIZON:]  # timestamps whose labels overlap test
train = X.iloc[:split_pos].drop(index=embargo_ts, errors="ignore") ...
```

Hmm, but test starts at split_pos; train rows with timestamps in the last LABEL_HORIZON 5m stamps before/at boundary have labels reaching into test. Embargo: remove train rows whose timestamp > (test_start_time - horizon*5m). Let me implement:

```python
def chronological_split(X, y, test_fraction=TEST_FRACTION, embargo_bars=LABEL_HORIZON):
    n = len(X)
    split_pos = int(n * (1 - test_fraction))
    test_start = X.index[split_pos]
    embargo_start = test_start - pd.Timedelta(minutes=5 * embargo_bars)
    train_mask = X.index < embargo_start
    test_mask = X.index >= test_start
    return X[train_mask], X[test_mask], y[train_mask], y[test_mask]
```

Wait — X.index here: after concat and sort_index, index is timestamps (not unique, duplicated across symbols). X.index[split_pos] is a Timestamp. train_mask = index < embargo_start. This drops rows between embargo_start and test_start (the embargo gap) — good, and also everything is time-based. 

But note: pooled sorted by timestamp — but I did `pooled = pd.concat(frames).sort_index()`. frames each have their own timestamps; concat then sort by index gives global time order. Good.

`_class_weights / scale_pos_weight`:

```python
def _scale_pos_weight(y):
    neg = int((y == 0).sum()); pos = int((y == 1).sum())
    return neg / max(pos, 1)
```

```python
    }

    return params
```

Optuna tuning for LightGBM:

```python
def tune_lightgbm(X_train, y_train, n_trials=N_OPTUNA_TRIALS, cv_splits=CV_SPLITS):
    import optuna
    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    spw = _scale_pos_weight(y_train)
    tscv = TimeSeriesSplit(n_splits=cv_splits)
    def objective(trial):
        params = _lgbm_params(
            spw,
            learning_rate=trial.suggest_float("learning_rate", 0.01, 0.2, log=True),
            num_leaves=trial.suggest_int("num_leaves", 15, 127),
            max_depth=trial.suggest_int("max_depth", 3, 8),
            min_child_samples=trial.suggest_int("min_child_samples", 20, 200),
            subsample=trial.suggest_float("subsample", 0.6, 1.0),
            colsample_bytree=trial.suggest_float("colsample_bytree", 0.6, 1.0),
            reg_lambda=trial.suggest_float("reg_lambda", 1e-2, 10.0, log=True),
            n_estimators=trial.suggest_int("n_estimators", 200, 800),
        )
        aucs = []
        for tr_idx, va_idx in tscv.split(X_train):
            model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**params)
            model.fit(X_train.iloc[tr_idx], y_train.iloc[tr_idx])
            proba = model.predict_proba(X_train.iloc[va_idx])[:, 1]
            aucs.append(roc_auc_score(y_train.iloc[va_idx], proba))
        return float(np.mean(aucs))
    study = optuna.create_study(direction="maximize")
    study.optimize(objective, n_trials=n_trials)
    logger.info("Best CV AUC %.4f with params: %s", study.best_value, study.best_params)
    return study.best_params
```

Evaluation:

```python
def evaluate(model, X_test, y_test, name):
    proba = model.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]
    pred = (proba >= 0.5).astype(int)
    metrics = {
        "accuracy": accuracy_score(y_test, pred),
        "balanced_accuracy": balanced_accuracy_score(y_test, pred),
        "precision": precision_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0),
        "recall": recall_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0),
        "f1": f1_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0),
        "roc_auc": roc_auc_score(y_test, proba) if y_test.nunique() > 1 else float("nan"),
        "log_loss": log_loss(y_test, proba),
        "brier": brier_score_loss(y_test, proba),
    }
    logger.info("%s test metrics: %s", name, {k: round(v,4) ...})
    return metrics
```

`train_models(symbols, data, features, tune=False)` orchestrates:

```python
def train_models(symbols, data, features, tune=False):
    X, y, meta = assemble_dataset(symbols, data, features)
    logger.info("Dataset: %d samples x %d features | class balance: %.1f%% up", ...)
    X_tr, X_te, y_tr, y_te = chronological_split(X, y)
    spw = _scale_pos_weight(y_tr)
    best_params = tune_lightgbm(X_tr, y_tr) if tune else {}
    lgb_model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**_lgbm_params(spw, **best_params))
    lgb_model.fit(X_tr, y_tr)
    ...
    xgb_model = xgb.XGBClassifier(**_xgb_params(spw))
    xgb_model.fit(X_tr, y_tr)
    metrics = {"lightgbm": evaluate(lgb_model, X_te, y_te, "LightGBM"), "xgboost": evaluate(...)}
    best_name = max(metrics, key=lambda k: metrics[k]["log_loss"] ... )
```

```python
def save_model(model, feature_cols, path):
    bundle = {"model": model, "feature_columns": feature_cols, "label_config": {...}}
    Path(path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    joblib.dump(bundle, path)
```

```python
def predict_latest(features_df, bundle):
    cols = bundle["feature_columns"]
    row = features_df[cols].iloc[[-1]].dropna()
    if row.empty: return None
    proba = bundle["model"].predict_proba(row)[0, 1]
    return proba
```

```python
def _col(df: Optional[pd.DataFrame], name: str, index) -> pd.Series:
    if df is None or name not in df.columns:
        return pd.Series(np.nan, index=index)
    return df[name]
```

```python
"""
feature_engineering.py
======================

Task 3: feature engineering for the ML scalping engine.

Consumes the output of data_pipeline.py:

    symbols: List[str]
    data:    {(symbol, timeframe): UTC-indexed OHLCV DataFrame}

and produces, for every symbol, a tidy feature DataFrame on the 5m index:

    features: {symbol: DataFrame}   # one row per 5m bar, one column per feature

Feature groups
--------------
5m (primary timeframe):
    * multi-horizon log returns
    * trend: EMA distances/slope (ATR-normalised), MACD, ADX/DMI
    * volatility: ATR, ATR%, Bollinger bandwidth + %B position
    * momentum/oscillators: RSI, Stochastic RSI
    * volume: z-score, OBV slope
    * candle anatomy: body ratio, close position, wicks, distance to
      rolling highs/lows (breakout context)

15m (higher timeframe, lookahead-safe):
    * return, RSI, ATR%, trend vs EMA, volume z-score
    * aligned onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift so that only fully
      closed 15m bars are ever used (no lookahead).

All features are computed from past data only and are scale-free
(ratios, ATR- or range-normalised) so they pool cleanly across symbols.

Usage (standalone smoke test):
    python feature_engineering.py

Usage (from other modules):
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    from feature_engineering import build_features
    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    features = build_features(symbols, data)
"""
```

Since no written task list was ever shared, I'm working from the strong hints in the repo itself: `data_pipeline.py`'s docstring says its output is shaped for "the feature-engineering module (next task)", and `requirements.txt` adds `pandas-ta` (indicators), `scikit-learn`/`xgboost`/`lightgbm`/`optuna`/`joblib` (ML) on top of the pipeline. So I've implemented:

- **Task 3 — `feature_engineering.py`**: turns the pipeline's OHLCV into per-symbol, scale-free feature matrices on the 5m index (returns, trend, volatility, oscillators, volume, candle anatomy) plus lookahead-safe 15m context features.
- **Task 4 — `ml_model.py`**: triple-barrier labeling (ATR-sized), pooled cross-sectional dataset, chronological split with embargo, LightGBM + XGBoost training (optional Optuna tuning), test-set evaluation, model persistence and an inference helper.

No changes are needed to `data_pipeline.py` or `requirements.txt` — both are consumed as-is.

feature_engineering.py
```
"""
feature_engineering.py
======================

Task 3: feature engineering for the ML scalping engine.

Consumes the output of data_pipeline.py:

    symbols: List[str]
    data:    {(symbol, timeframe): UTC-indexed OHLCV DataFrame}

and produces, for every symbol, a tidy feature DataFrame on the 5m index:

    features: {symbol: DataFrame}   # one row per 5m bar, one column per feature

Feature groups
--------------
5m (primary timeframe):
    * multi-horizon log returns
    * trend: EMA distances/slope (ATR-normalised), MACD, ADX/DMI
    * volatility: ATR, ATR%, Bollinger bandwidth + %B position
    * oscillators: RSI, Stochastic RSI
    * volume: z-score, OBV slope
    * candle anatomy: body ratio, close position, wicks, distance to
      rolling highs/lows (breakout context)

15m (higher timeframe, lookahead-safe):
    * return, RSI, ATR%, trend vs EMA, volume z-score
    * aligned onto the 5m index with a one-bar shift so that only fully
      closed 15m bars are ever used (no lookahead).

All features are computed from past data only and are scale-free
(ratios, ATR- or range-normalised) so they pool cleanly across symbols
in the ML stage (Task 4).

Usage (standalone smoke test):
    python feature_engineering.py

Usage (from other modules):
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data
    from feature_engineering import build_features
    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    features = build_features(symbols, data)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import asyncio
import logging
from typing import Dict, List, Optional, Tuple

import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import pandas_ta as ta

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

PRIMARY_TF = "5m"           # timeframe the feature rows live on
HTF_TF = "15m"              # higher timeframe used for context features

# --- indicator parameters (5m) ---
RSI_LEN = 14
ATR_LEN = 14
ADX_LEN = 14
EMA_FAST = 9
EMA_SLOW = 21
EMA_TREND = 50
MACD_FAST, MACD_SLOW, MACD_SIGNAL = 12, 26, 9
BB_LEN, BB_STD = 20, 2.0
STOCHRSI_LEN = 14
VOL_Z_WIN = 50              # rolling window for volume z-score
RETURN_HORIZONS = (1, 3, 5, 15)   # log-return horizons, in 5m bars
ROLL_HIGHLOW_WIN = 24       # rolling high/low window (2h of 5m bars)

# Raw OHLCV columns are kept in the frames for convenience (labeling needs
# high/low/close) but are NOT features - see feature_columns().
RAW_COLUMNS = ("open", "high", "low", "close", "volume")

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("feature_engineering")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Helpers
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _rename_by_prefix(df: pd.DataFrame, mapping: Dict[str, str]) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Rename pandas-ta output columns (e.g. 'MACDh_12_26_9') to stable names.

    Longer prefixes must come first in `mapping` because iteration stops at
    the first matching prefix ('MACDh' before 'MACD', etc.).
    """
    renamed = {}
    for col in df.columns:
        for prefix, name in mapping.items():
            if col.startswith(prefix):
                renamed[col] = name
                break
    return df.rename(columns=renamed)


def _col(
    df: Optional[pd.DataFrame], name: str, index: pd.Index
) -> pd.Series:
    """Fetch a column by name, or an all-NaN series if absent (robustness)."""
    if df is None or name not in df.columns:
        return pd.Series(np.nan, index=index)
    return df[name]


def feature_columns(df: pd.DataFrame) -> List[str]:
    """Columns of `df` that are usable as ML features (raw OHLCV excluded)."""
    return [c for c in df.columns if c not in RAW_COLUMNS]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 5m indicator block
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def add_indicators(df: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """Compute all 5m features on a copy of the OHLCV frame."""
    out = df.copy()
    close, high, low = out["close"], out["high"], out["low"]
    open_, volume = out["open"], out["volume"]

    # --- returns / momentum -------------------------------------------------
    for h in RETURN_HORIZONS:
        out[f"ret_{h}"] = np.log(close / close.shift(h))
    roc = ta.roc(close, length=10)
    out["roc_10"] = np.nan if roc is None else roc / 100.0  # ta.roc is in %

    # --- trend ---------------------------------------------------------------
    atr = ta.atr(high, low, close, length=ATR_LEN)
    out["atr"] = atr
    out["atr_pct"] = atr / close

    out["ema_fast"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_FAST)
    out["ema_slow"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_SLOW)
    out["ema_trend"] = ta.ema(close, length=EMA_TREND)
    # ATR-normalised distances keep features scale-free across symbols.
    out["close_to_ema_fast"] = (close - out["ema_fast"]) / atr
    out["close_to_ema_slow"] = (close - out["ema_slow"]) / atr
    out["ema_fast_slope"] = out["ema_fast"].diff(3) / atr

    macd = ta.macd(close, fast=MACD_FAST, slow=MACD_SLOW, signal=MACD_SIGNAL)
    macd = _rename_by_prefix(
        macd, {"MACDh": "macd_hist", "MACDs": "macd_signal", "MACD": "macd"}
    )
    out["macd"] = _col(macd, "macd", out.index) / close
    out["macd_signal"] = _col(macd, "macd_signal", out.index) / close
    out["macd_hist"] = _col(macd, "macd_hist", out.index) / close

    adx = ta.adx(high, low, close, length=ADX_LEN)
    adx = _rename_by_prefix(adx, {"ADX": "adx", "DMP": "di_plus", "DMN": "di_minus"})
    out["adx"] = _col(adx, "adx", out.index)
    out["di_plus"] = _col(adx, "di_plus", out.index)
    out["di_minus"] = _col(adx, "di_minus", out.index)

    # --- volatility bands ------------------------------------------------------
    bb = ta.bbands(close, length=BB_LEN, std=BB_STD)
    bb = _rename_by_prefix(
        bb,
        {
            "BBL": "bb_low", "BBM": "bb_mid", "BBU": "bb_high",
            "BBB": "bb_width", "BBP": "bb_pctl",
        },
    )
    out["bb_pctl"] = _col(bb, "bb_pctl", out.index)          # 0..1 in-band position
    out["bb_width"] = _col(bb, "bb_width", out.index) / 100.0  # % -> fraction

    # --- oscillators ------------------------------------------------------------
    out["rsi"] = ta.rsi(close, length=RSI_LEN)
    stoch = ta.stochrsi(close, length=STOCHRSI_LEN)
    stoch = _rename_by_prefix(
        stoch, {"STOCHRSIk": "stochrsi_k", "STOCHRSId": "stochrsi_d"}
    )
    out["stochrsi_k"] = _col(stoch, "stochrsi_k", out.index)
    out["stochrsi_d"] = _col(stoch, "stochrsi_d", out.index)

    # --- volume -------------------------------------------------------------------
    vol_mean = volume.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).mean()
    vol_std = volume.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).std()
    out["vol_z"] = (volume - vol_mean) / (vol_std + 1e-12)
    obv = ta.obv(close, volume)
    out["obv_slope"] = (
        np.nan if obv is None
        else obv.diff(5) / (volume.rolling(5).sum() + 1e-12)
    )

    # --- candle anatomy (price action) ---------------------------------------------
    rng = (high - low).replace(0, np.nan)
    out["body_ratio"] = (close - open_) / rng                # -1..1
    out["close_pos_in_bar"] = (close - low) / rng            # 0..1
    out["upper_wick"] = (high - np.maximum(close, open_)) / rng
    out["lower_wick"] = (np.minimum(close, open_) - low) / rng
    # Distance to recent extremes in ATR units (breakout context).
    out["dist_to_high_24"] = (close - high.rolling(ROLL_HIGHLOW_WIN).max()) / atr
    out["dist_to_low_24"] = (close - low.rolling(ROLL_HIGHLOW_WIN).min()) / atr

    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# 15m context block (lookahead-safe)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def add_htf_context(df_5m: pd.DataFrame, df_15m: pd.DataFrame) -> pd.DataFrame:
    """
    Merge higher-timeframe context onto the 5m index without lookahead.

    A 15m bar stamped T only *closes* at T+15m, so the whole context frame is
    shifted one 15m bar forward before alignment. After the shift, the value
    aligned to a 5m timestamp T is the context of the last 15m bar that fully
    closed strictly before T.
    """
    out = df_5m.copy()
    h_close, h_high, h_low, h_vol = (
        df_15m["close"], df_15m["high"], df_15m["low"], df_15m["volume"],
    )
    h_atr = ta.atr(h_high, h_low, h_close, length=ATR_LEN)

    ctx = pd.DataFrame(index=df_15m.index)
    ctx["htf_ret_1"] = np.log(h_close / h_close.shift(1))
    ctx["htf_rsi"] = ta.rsi(h_close, length=RSI_LEN)
    ctx["htf_atr_pct"] = h_atr / h_close
    h_ema = ta.ema(h_close, length=EMA_SLOW)
    ctx["htf_trend"] = (h_close - h_ema) / h_atr
    h_mean = h_vol.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).mean()
    h_std = h_vol.rolling(VOL_Z_WIN).std()
    ctx["htf_vol_z"] = (h_vol - h_mean) / (h_std + 1e-12)

    ctx = ctx.shift(1).reindex(out.index, method="ffill")
    for col in ctx.columns:
        out[col] = ctx[col]
    return out


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Orchestration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def build_features(
    symbols: List[str],
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
    dropna: bool = True,
) -> Dict[str, pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Build the feature matrix for every symbol.

    Returns {symbol: DataFrame} indexed by the 5m UTC timestamp. With
    dropna=True the indicator warm-up rows (leading NaNs) are removed.
    """
    features: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame] = {}

    for symbol in symbols:
        df_5m = data.get((symbol, PRIMARY_TF))
        df_15m = data.get((symbol, HTF_TF))

        if df_5m is None or df_5m.empty:
            logger.warning("%s: no %s data, skipping", symbol, PRIMARY_TF)
            continue

        feat = add_indicators(df_5m)

        if df_15m is not None and not df_15m.empty:
            feat = add_htf_context(feat, df_15m)
        else:
            logger.warning("%s: no %s data, HTF context skipped", symbol, HTF_TF)

        if dropna:
            before = len(feat)
            feat = feat.dropna()
            logger.debug(
                "%s: dropped %d warm-up rows", symbol, before - len(feat)
            )

        if feat.empty:
            logger.warning("%s: no complete feature rows, skipping", symbol)
            continue

        features[symbol] = feat
        logger.info(
            "%s: %d rows x %d features",
            symbol, len(feat), len(feature_columns(feat)),
        )

    if not features:
        raise RuntimeError("No features could be built - check pipeline output.")
    return features


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Standalone smoke test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

async def _main() -> None:
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data

    symbols, data = await load_market_data()
    features = build_features(symbols, data)

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print("FEATURE ENGINEERING SUMMARY (5m primary + 15m context)")
    print("=" * 78)
    for i, symbol in enumerate(symbols, start=1):
        feat = features.get(symbol)
        if feat is None:
            print(f"{i:>2}. {symbol:<14} MISSING")
            continue
        print(
            f"{i:>2}. {symbol:<14} {len(feat):>4} rows x "
            f"{len(feature_columns(feat)):>3} features "
            f"[{feat.index[0]:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} -> {feat.index[-1]:%Y-%m-%d %H:%M} UTC]"
        )
    print("=" * 78)

    first_symbol = next(iter(features))
    cols = feature_columns(features[first_symbol])
    print(f"\nFeature columns ({len(cols)}):")
    for c in cols:
        print(f"  - {c}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main())
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted by user.")
```

ml_model.py
```
"""
ml_model.py
===========

Task 4: labeling + model training for the ML scalping engine.

Pipeline:
    1. Triple-barrier labels on the 5m bars (profit-take / stop-loss / time
       barrier), sized in ATR so the target is volatility-normalised.
    2. Pool all symbols into one cross-sectional dataset (features are
       scale-free, so symbols can be trained on jointly).
    3. Strictly chronological train/test split with an embargo gap so no
       label window straddles the boundary (no lookahead leakage).
    4. Train LightGBM and XGBoost classifiers (class imbalance handled via
       scale_pos_weight), optionally tune LightGBM with Optuna over
       TimeSeriesSplit CV.
    5. Evaluate both on the untouched test tail, keep the best model by
       ROC AUC, and persist it (joblib) together with its feature list and
       the label configuration.

Usage (standalone smoke test):
    python ml_model.py            # quick train with default hyper-parameters
    python ml_model.py --tune     # + Optuna hyper-parameter search (slower)

Usage (from other modules):
    from ml_model import load_model, predict_latest
    bundle = load_model("models/scalp_model.joblib")
    proba_up = predict_latest(features["BTC/USDT"], bundle)
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import asyncio
import logging
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, Tuple

import joblib
import lightgbm as lgb
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd
import xgboost as xgb
from sklearn.metrics import (
    accuracy_score,
    balanced_accuracy_score,
    brier_score_loss,
    f1_score,
    log_loss,
    precision_score,
    recall_score,
    roc_auc_score,
)
from sklearn.model_selection import TimeSeriesSplit

from feature_engineering import PRIMARY_TF, RAW_COLUMNS, build_features

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Configuration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

MODEL_DIR = "models"
MODEL_FILENAME = "scalp_model.joblib"
DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH = str(Path(MODEL_DIR) / MODEL_FILENAME)

# --- labeling: triple-barrier ------------------------------------------------
PT_ATR_MULT = 1.0        # profit-take barrier: +PT_ATR_MULT x ATR above entry
SL_ATR_MULT = 1.0        # stop-loss barrier:   -SL_ATR_MULT x ATR below entry
LABEL_HORIZON = 6        # time barrier: 6 x 5m bars = 30 minutes

# --- training ------------------------------------------------------------------
TEST_FRACTION = 0.2      # last 20% of the pooled timeline = test set
EMBARGO_BARS = LABEL_HORIZON   # gap (in 5m bars) between train and test
CV_SPLITS = 3            # TimeSeriesSplit folds used by Optuna
N_OPTUNA_TRIALS = 25
RANDOM_STATE = 42

logging.basicConfig(
    level=logging.INFO,
    format="%(asctime)s | %(levelname)-7s | %(name)s | %(message)s",
)
logger = logging.getLogger("ml_model")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Labeling: triple-barrier
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def triple_barrier_labels(
    ohlcv: pd.DataFrame,
    atr: pd.Series,
    pt_mult: float = PT_ATR_MULT,
    sl_mult: float = SL_ATR_MULT,
    horizon: int = LABEL_HORIZON,
) -> pd.Series:
    """
    Triple-barrier labels on 5m bars (entry = close of bar t).

        upper barrier = close[t] + pt_mult * atr[t]   -> label 1 (profit-take)
        lower barrier = close[t] - sl_mult * atr[t]   -> label 0 (stop-loss)
        time barrier  = horizon bars later

    Scan bars t+1 .. t+horizon in order:
        * high >= upper first            -> 1
        * low  <= lower first            -> 0
        * both hit inside the same bar   -> 0 (conservative: assume worst case)
        * neither hit within the horizon -> 1 if close[t+horizon] > close[t] else 0

    Bars without a full forward window or with NaN/zero ATR get NaN labels.
    """
    n = len(ohlcv)
    labels = np.full(n, np.nan)
    close = ohlcv["close"].to_numpy()
    high = ohlcv["high"].to_numpy()
    low = ohlcv["low"].to_numpy()
    atr_arr = atr.to_numpy()

    for t in range(n - horizon):
        a = atr_arr[t]
        if np.isnan(a) or a <= 0:
            continue
        upper = close[t] + pt_mult * a
        lower = close[t] - sl_mult * a

        label = np.nan
        for k in range(1, horizon + 1):
            hit_up = high[t + k] >= upper
            hit_dn = low[t + k] <= lower
            if hit_up and hit_dn:
                label = 0.0          # ambiguous bar -> conservative stop
                break
            if hit_up:
                label = 1.0
                break
            if hit_dn:
                label = 0.0
                break
        if np.isnan(label):
            label = 1.0 if close[t + horizon] > close[t] else 0.0
        labels[t] = label

    return pd.Series(labels, index=ohlcv.index, name="label")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Dataset assembly
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def assemble_dataset(
    symbols: list,
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
    features: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
) -> Tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.Series, pd.DataFrame]:
    """
    Pool every symbol into one labeled dataset.

    Returns (X, y, meta) where meta carries the symbol per row for traceability.
    """
    exclude = list(RAW_COLUMNS) + ["label", "symbol"]
    frames = []

    for symbol in symbols:
        feat = features.get(symbol)
        ohlcv = data.get((symbol, PRIMARY_TF))
        if feat is None or feat.empty or ohlcv is None:
            continue
        df = feat.copy()
        df["label"] = triple_barrier_labels(ohlcv, feat["atr"])
        df["symbol"] = symbol
        frames.append(df)

    if not frames:
        raise RuntimeError("No labeled samples could be assembled.")

    pooled = pd.concat(frames).sort_index()
    pooled = pooled.dropna(subset=["label"])

    feature_cols = [c for c in pooled.columns if c not in exclude]
    X = pooled[feature_cols].dropna()
    y = pooled["label"].astype(int).loc[X.index]
    meta = pooled.loc[X.index, ["symbol"]]

    logger.info(
        "Dataset: %d samples x %d features | up-share %.1f%%",
        len(X), X.shape[1], 100.0 * y.mean(),
    )
    return X, y, meta


def chronological_split(
    X: pd.DataFrame,
    y: pd.Series,
    test_fraction: float = TEST_FRACTION,
    embargo_bars: int = EMBARGO_BARS,
) -> Tuple[pd.DataFrame, pd.DataFrame, pd.Series, pd.Series]:
    """
    Time-ordered split with an embargo gap.

    The embargo removes training rows whose label window (LABEL_HORIZON bars
    into the future) would overlap the test period.
    """
    n = len(X)
    split_pos = int(n * (1.0 - test_fraction))
    if split_pos <= 0 or split_pos >= n:
        raise RuntimeError("Not enough samples for a chronological split.")

    test_start = X.index[split_pos]
    embargo_start = test_start - pd.Timedelta(PRIMARY_TF) * embargo_bars

    train_mask = X.index < embargo_start
    test_mask = X.index >= test_start

    X_tr, y_tr = X[train_mask], y[train_mask]
    X_te, y_te = X[test_mask], y[test_mask]

    if X_tr.empty or X_te.empty:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Empty train or test split - lower TEST_FRACTION or fetch more data."
        )
    logger.info(
        "Split: %d train / %d test rows (embargo from %s to %s)",
        len(X_tr), len(X_te), embargo_start, test_start,
    )
    return X_tr, X_te, y_tr, y_te


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Models
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _scale_pos_weight(y: pd.Series) -> float:
    neg = int((y == 0).sum())
    pos = int((y == 1).sum())
    return neg / max(pos, 1)


def _lgbm_params(scale_pos_weight: float, **overrides) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    params: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "objective": "binary",
        "learning_rate": 0.05,
        "num_leaves": 31,
        "max_depth": -1,
        "min_child_samples": 40,
        "subsample": 0.9,
        "subsample_freq": 1,
        "colsample_bytree": 0.9,
        "reg_lambda": 1.0,
        "n_estimators": 400,
        "scale_pos_weight": scale_pos_weight,
        "random_state": RANDOM_STATE,
        "n_jobs": -1,
        "verbosity": -1,
    }
    params.update(overrides)
    return params


def _xgb_params(scale_pos_weight: float, **overrides) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    params: Dict[str, Any] = {
        "objective": "binary:logistic",
        "eval_metric": "logloss",
        "learning_rate": 0.05,
        "max_depth": 6,
        "min_child_weight": 5,
        "subsample": 0.9,
        "colsample_bytree": 0.9,
        "reg_lambda": 1.0,
        "n_estimators": 400,
        "scale_pos_weight": scale_pos_weight,
        "random_state": RANDOM_STATE,
        "n_jobs": -1,
        "tree_method": "hist",
    }
    params.update(overrides)
    return params


def tune_lightgbm(
    X_train: pd.DataFrame,
    y_train: pd.Series,
    n_trials: int = N_OPTUNA_TRIALS,
    cv_splits: int = CV_SPLITS,
) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Optuna search maximising mean ROC AUC over TimeSeriesSplit CV."""
    import optuna  # imported lazily: only needed when tuning is requested

    optuna.logging.set_verbosity(optuna.logging.WARNING)
    spw = _scale_pos_weight(y_train)
    tscv = TimeSeriesSplit(n_splits=cv_splits)

    def objective(trial: "optuna.Trial") -> float:
        params = _lgbm_params(
            spw,
            learning_rate=trial.suggest_float("learning_rate", 0.01, 0.2, log=True),
            num_leaves=trial.suggest_int("num_leaves", 15, 127),
            max_depth=trial.suggest_int("max_depth", 3, 8),
            min_child_samples=trial.suggest_int("min_child_samples", 20, 200),
            subsample=trial.suggest_float("subsample", 0.6, 1.0),
            colsample_bytree=trial.suggest_float("colsample_bytree", 0.6, 1.0),
            reg_lambda=trial.suggest_float("reg_lambda", 1e-2, 10.0, log=True),
            n_estimators=trial.suggest_int("n_estimators", 200, 800),
        )
        aucs = []
        for tr_idx, va_idx in tscv.split(X_train):
            model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**params)
            model.fit(X_train.iloc[tr_idx], y_train.iloc[tr_idx])
            proba = model.predict_proba(X_train.iloc[va_idx])[:, 1]
            aucs.append(roc_auc_score(y_train.iloc[va_idx], proba))
        return float(np.mean(aucs))

    study = optuna.create_study(direction="maximize")
    study.optimize(objective, n_trials=n_trials)
    logger.info(
        "Optuna best CV AUC %.4f with params: %s",
        study.best_value, study.best_params,
    )
    return study.best_params


def evaluate(model: Any, X_test: pd.DataFrame, y_test: pd.Series, name: str) -> Dict[str, float]:
    """Compute standard binary-classification metrics on the test set."""
    proba = model.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]
    pred = (proba >= 0.5).astype(int)
    metrics = {
        "accuracy": float(accuracy_score(y_test, pred)),
        "balanced_accuracy": float(balanced_accuracy_score(y_test, pred)),
        "precision": float(precision_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0)),
        "recall": float(recall_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0)),
        "f1": float(f1_score(y_test, pred, zero_division=0)),
        "roc_auc": (
            float(roc_auc_score(y_test, proba))
            if y_test.nunique() > 1 else float("nan")
        ),
        "log_loss": float(log_loss(y_test, proba)),
        "brier": float(brier_score_loss(y_test, proba)),
    }
    pretty = {k: (round(v, 4) if not pd.isna(v) else v) for k, v in metrics.items()}
    logger.info("%s test metrics: %s", name, pretty)
    return metrics


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Persistence & inference
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def save_model(
    model: Any,
    feature_cols: list,
    metrics: Dict[str, float],
    path: str = DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH,
) -> str:
    """Persist model + feature list + label config as a single joblib bundle."""
    bundle = {
        "model": model,
        "feature_columns": list(feature_cols),
        "label_config": {
            "pt_atr_mult": PT_ATR_MULT,
            "sl_atr_mult": SL_ATR_MULT,
            "horizon": LABEL_HORIZON,
        },
        "test_metrics": metrics,
        "trained_at": pd.Timestamp.utcnow().isoformat(),
    }
    Path(path).parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    joblib.dump(bundle, path)
    logger.info("Saved best model to %s", path)
    return path


def load_model(path: str = DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Load a bundle written by save_model()."""
    return joblib.load(path)


def predict_latest(features_df: pd.DataFrame, bundle: Dict[str, Any]) -> float:
    """
    Probability of an 'up' outcome for the most recent bar of a feature frame.

    Returns NaN if the latest row is incomplete (missing feature values).
    """
    cols = bundle["feature_columns"]
    row = features_df[cols].iloc[[-1]]
    if row.isna().to_numpy().any():
        logger.warning("Latest feature row is incomplete - returning NaN.")
        return float("nan")
    return float(bundle["model"].predict_proba(row)[0, 1])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Orchestration
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def train_models(
    symbols: list,
    data: Dict[Tuple[str, str], pd.DataFrame],
    features: Dict[str, pd.DataFrame],
    tune: bool = False,
    model_path: str = DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH,
) -> Tuple[str, Any, Dict[str, Dict[str, float]]]:
    """
    Label, split, train LightGBM + XGBoost, evaluate, and persist the best.

    Returns (best_name, best_model, metrics_by_model).
    """
    X, y, _meta = assemble_dataset(symbols, data, features)
    X_tr, X_te, y_tr, y_te = chronological_split(X, y)
    spw = _scale_pos_weight(y_tr)

    # --- LightGBM (optionally tuned) -----------------------------------------
    best_params = tune_lightgbm(X_tr, y_tr) if tune else {}
    lgb_model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**_lgbm_params(spw, **best_params))
    lgb_model.fit(X_tr, y_tr)

    # --- XGBoost (sane defaults, no tuning) ------------------------------------
    xgb_model = xgb.XGBClassifier(**_xgb_params(spw))
    xgb_model.fit(X_tr, y_tr)

    # --- evaluation & model selection -------------------------------------------
    metrics = {
        "lightgbm": evaluate(lgb_model, X_te, y_te, "LightGBM"),
        "xgboost": evaluate(xgb_model, X_te, y_te, "XGBoost"),
    }

    def _auc(m: Dict[str, float]) -> float:
        v = m.get("roc_auc", float("nan"))
        return 0.5 if pd.isna(v) else v

    best_name = max(metrics, key=lambda n: _auc(metrics[n]))
    best_model = lgb_model if best_name == "lightgbm" else xgb_model
    logger.info("Best model by ROC AUC: %s", best_name)

    feature_cols = list(X.columns)
    if hasattr(best_model, "feature_importances_"):
        importances = pd.Series(
            best_model.feature_importances_, index=feature_cols
        ).sort_values(ascending=False)
        logger.info(
            "Top 15 features (%s):\n%s", best_name, importances.head(15).to_string()
        )

    save_model(best_model, feature_cols, metrics[best_name], model_path)
    return best_name, best_model, metrics


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Standalone smoke test
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

async def _main(tune: bool = False) -> None:
    from data_pipeline import load_market_data

    logger.info("Stage 1/3: downloading market data ...")
    symbols, data = await load_market_data()

    logger.info("Stage 2/3: building features ...")
    features = build_features(symbols, data)

    logger.info("Stage 3/3: labeling + training ...")
    best_name, _model, metrics = train_models(symbols, data, features, tune=tune)

    print("\n" + "=" * 78)
    print("TASK 4 SMOKE TEST - TEST-SET COMPARISON")
    print("=" * 78)
    header = f"{'model':<12} {'accuracy':>9} {'precision':>10} {'recall':>8} {'f1':>7} {'roc_auc':>8} {'logloss':>8}"
    print(header)
    print("-" * len(header))
    for name, m in metrics.items():
        auc = m["roc_auc"]
        auc_str = f"{auc:>8.4f}" if not pd.isna(auc) else f"{'n/a':>8}"
        print(
            f"{name:<12} {m['accuracy']:>9.4f} {m['precision']:>10.4f} "
            f"{m['recall']:>8.4f} {m['f1']:>7.4f} {auc_str} {m['log_loss']:>8.4f}"
        )
    print("-" * len(header))
    print(f"Best model: {best_name} (saved to {DEFAULT_MODEL_PATH})")
    print("=" * 78)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(
        description="Task 4 smoke test: triple-barrier labels + model training"
    )
    parser.add_argument(
        "--tune", action="store_true",
        help="run the Optuna hyper-parameter search for LightGBM (slower)",
    )
    args = parser.parse_args()
    try:
        asyncio.run(_main(tune=args.tune))
    except KeyboardInterrupt:
        print("\nInterrupted by user.")
```
